<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the <i>P</i> = <i>W</i> conjecture on the full rational cohomology of fixed-determinant, trace-free Higgs moduli spaces in composite rank and coprime degree, for smooth projective complex curves of genus at least two. Together with the established prime-rank cases, this gives the equality in every coprime rank.

- **C01** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every smooth projective complex curve $C$ of genus $g\ge2$, every composite integer $n\ge4$, every integer $d$ with $\gcd(n,d)=1$, and every $L\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$, one has $$P_kH^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)=W_{2k}H^m(X^B,\mathbb Q) =W_{2k+1}H^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)$$ for all $m,k\ge0$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the perverse and weight filtrations on full cohomology in composite coprime rank.
- **C02** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-norm-dimensions)): Set $R=(n^2-1)(g-1)$. Then $\dim X=2R$, $\dim A=R$, and every fiber of $h$ has dimension $R$. In particular, $$r:=\dim(X\times_A X)-\dim X=R.$$
  - Explanation: Establishes the dimensions and equidimensionality needed to normalize the Hitchin filtration.
- **C03** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-norm-low-cohomology)): For the composite ranks $n\ge4$ under consideration, $$H^0(X)=\mathbb Q,\qquad H^1(X)=0,\qquad H^2(X)=\mathbb Q e, \qquad e\ne0.$$ Every ample class on $X$ is a nonzero scalar multiple of $e$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the unique degree-two raiser and rules out low-degree variant contributions.
- **C04** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-norm-relative-lefschetz)): Cup product by $e$ sends $P_kH^m(X)$ into $P_{k+2}H^{m+2}(X)$. For every $i\ge0$ and every $m$, it induces an isomorphism $$\label{norm:relative-hl} e^i:\operatorname{Gr}^P_{R-i}H^m(X) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \operatorname{Gr}^P_{R+i}H^{m+2i}(X).$$
  - Explanation: Supplies relative hard Lefschetz for the normalized perverse filtration.
- **C05** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-alg-comparison)): Let $H$ be a finite-dimensional vector space over a field of characteristic zero, with finite, exhaustive, separated increasing integer-indexed filtrations $F_\bullet$ and $G_\bullet$. Suppose that $e,f\in\operatorname{End}(H)$ satisfy, for $A_\bullet=F_\bullet$ and $A_\bullet=G_\bullet$, $$e(A_k)\subseteq A_{k+2},\qquad f(A_k)\subseteq A_{k-2}.$$ Assume that the induced raiser on each associated graded is Lefschetz with the same center $r$, and that $$\label{alg:double-commutator} [[f,e],e]=-2e, \qquad [u,v]=uv-vu.$$ Then $F_k=G_k$ for every integer $k$. If $H$ has a further grading respected by both filtrations, and $e,f$ have degrees $2,-2$ for that grading, the equality holds degree by degree.
  - Explanation: Determines both filtrations from the same raiser and lowering operator, including extensions.
- **C06** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-alg-tensor)): Let $V,W$ be nonzero finite integer-graded vector spaces over a field of characteristic zero, and let $E_V,E_W$ have degree two. If $E_V\otimes1+1\otimes E_W$ is Lefschetz with center $r$ on $V\otimes W$, then $E_V$ and $E_W$ are Lefschetz with centers $r_V,r_W$, respectively, where $r_V+r_W=r$.
  - Explanation: Cancels auxiliary tensor factors from a Lefschetz symmetry.
- **C07** (theorem, [source](#stmt-betti-lefschetz)): The mixed Hodge structure on $H^*(X^{\mathrm B},\mathbb Q)$ is Hodge–Tate. Under the nonabelian-Hodge identification, the class $e$ has nonzero image in $\operatorname{Gr}^W_4H^2(X^{\mathrm B},\mathbb Q)$, and $$e^i:\operatorname{Gr}^W_{2R-2i}H^m(X^{\mathrm B},\mathbb Q) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \operatorname{Gr}^W_{2R+2i}H^{m+2i}(X^{\mathrm B},\mathbb Q) \qquad(i\geq0).$$ In particular, $W_{2k}=W_{2k+1}$ on this cohomology. These assertions concern the entire cohomology, including all nontrivial $\Gamma$-character summands after complexification.
  - Explanation: Supplies curious hard Lefschetz and even weights on full Betti cohomology.
- **C08** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-betti-geometry)): The morphism $Y^{\mathrm B}\to V$ is smooth of relative dimension $2R+2b$. There is a correspondence $$X^{\mathrm B}\xleftarrow{\ \pi\ }Z^{\mathrm B} \xrightarrow{\ \iota\ }Y^{\mathrm B}_q,$$ where $Z^{\mathrm B}$ imposes $\nu=\zeta I$ and $\pi$ is the full flag bundle. The map $\iota$ is a regular embedding of codimension $b$ with normal bundle $T_\pi^*$. The cohomological operators $$\mathsf A=\pi_*\iota^*,\qquad \mathsf B=\iota_*\pi^*$$ have degrees $-2b$ and $2b$, respectively, and satisfy $$\label{betti:split} \mathsf A\mathsf B=(-1)^b n!\,\mathrm{id}.$$ Here and below a covariant star denotes the Gysin map.
  - Explanation: Constructs a split flag correspondence with controlled degree shifts.
- **C09** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-betti-finite-cover)): Pulling the preceding auxiliary bundles and stratifications back along <a href="#betti:cover" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="betti:cover">[betti:cover]</a> has the following consequences.* 1. *Every fiber of $\widehat Y^{\mathrm B}\to V$ and of $Y^{\mathrm B}\to V$ has Hodge–Tate cohomology. For $\widehat Y^{\mathrm B}$ at a parameter with distinct eigenvalues, the pulled-back class satisfies curious hard Lefschetz on ordinary cohomology with half-weight center $\delta/2=R+b+g$.* 2. *The cohomology sheaves of both ordinary and compact-support integral direct images over $V$ are locally constant and finitely generated. Ordinary direct image commutes with restriction to every fiber. The same assertions hold for $Y^{\mathrm B}\to V$.
  - Explanation: Retains all lifted strata and proves ordinary base change without assuming properness of the family.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-diag-variation)): Under the preceding hypotheses, $H^j(\mathscr H_t,\mathbb Q)$ carries a graded-polarizable mixed Hodge structure, functorial for maps of flat bundles that are algebraic in flat trivializations. As $t$ varies, these structures form an admissible graded-polarizable variation of mixed Hodge structures, with an integral underlying local system up to torsion. They are computed by $H^j(i_t^*K_{\mathscr H})$.* *If $H^j(H_t,\mathbb Q)$ is Hodge–Tate for every $j$ and $t$, then $H^j(\mathscr H_t,\mathbb Q)$ is Hodge–Tate as well. We call its weight filtration the *diagram weight filtration*.
  - Explanation: Builds diagram mixed Hodge structures and their variations from algebraic fiber diagrams.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-diag-gysin)): The diagram weight filtrations have the following properties.* 1. *For the product $S\times M$, with $M$ a complex algebraic variety, the ordinary Künneth isomorphism identifies its mixed Hodge structure with that on $$H^*(S,\mathbb Q)\otimes H^*(M,\mathbb Q),$$ where every $H^a(S,\mathbb Q)$ is pure of weight zero and type $(0,0)$, and $H^*(M,\mathbb Q)$ has its Deligne mixed Hodge structure. If $S$ is oriented and closed of real dimension $d_S$, projection pullback and integration over $S$ preserve weight; the latter has cohomological degree $-d_S$.* 2. *Pullbacks by maps of flat bundles preserve weight.* 3. *Suppose $u:\mathscr H_t\to\mathscr H'_t$ is a proper map of flat bundles with smooth equidimensional algebraic fibers. Put $c=\dim_{\mathbb C}H'_t-\dim_{\mathbb C}H_t$. With the complex orientations on the fibers, its ordinary Gysin map satisfies $$u_*:W_aH^j(\mathscr H_t)\longrightarrow W_{a+2c}H^{j+2c}(\mathscr H'_t).$$ If $S$ is oriented, this is the Gysin map for the induced orientations on the total spaces.
  - Explanation: Separates weight-preserving base integration from the Tate shifts of fiber Gysin maps.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-diag-finite)): Assume the Hodge–Tate hypothesis of Proposition <a href="#diag:variation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="diag:variation">10</a>. The combined monodromy action on all $\operatorname{Gr}^W_aH^j(\mathscr H_t,\mathbb Q)$ has finite image. For any loop with cohomology monodromy $T$, there is an integer $M>0$ such that $T^M$ is unipotent and $$\label{diag:log} N=\frac{1}{M}\log(T^M) \quad\text{satisfies}\quad N(W_a)\subset W_{a-2}.$$ The logarithm is a finite polynomial. On the total cohomology ring, $N$ is a derivation of cohomological degree zero.
  - Explanation: Uses integral lattices and polarizations to obtain a weight-lowering monodromy logarithm.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-log-scalar-product)): Tensoring by $(J,D_{J,\lambda})$ gives an algebraic isomorphism $\mathcal O\simeq S\times\mathcal X$ over $S\times\mathbb A^1$. At $\lambda=0$ it preserves the ordinary Hitchin coefficients.
  - Explanation: Corrects the moving determinant by tensoring and identifies the scalar family as a product.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-log-families)): The morphism $\mathcal Y\to S\times\Lambda$ is smooth of relative dimension $2R+2b$, and $\mathcal X\to\mathbb A^1$ is smooth of relative dimension $2R$. Their total spaces are semiprojective for scaling the operators, which scales $\lambda$ and every $u_i$ with weight one. The same holds for the inverse image in $\mathcal Y$ of every linear subspace of $\Lambda$ through zero. In Figure <a href="#log:scalar-correspondence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:scalar-correspondence">1</a>, $\pi$ is a smooth proper flag projection of relative dimension $b$, and $\iota$ is a regular embedding of codimension $b$ with normal bundle $T_\pi^*$.
  - Explanation: Checks deformation smoothness, scaling limits, and the scalar embedding normal bundle.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-log-restriction)): For every $(\lambda,u)\in\Lambda$, restriction induces an isomorphism $$\rho_{\lambda,u}:H^*(\mathcal Y,\mathbb Q) \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }H^*(Y_{\lambda,u},\mathbb Q). \label{log:rho}$$ Restriction to the inverse image of any line through zero in $\Lambda$ is also an isomorphism, as is restriction from that inverse image to any of its fibers. The analogous restriction isomorphisms hold for all four families in Figure <a href="#log:scalar-correspondence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:scalar-correspondence">1</a> over the $\lambda$-line. They commute with pullbacks and with the proper Gysin maps in that correspondence, including integration in the $S$ factor.
  - Explanation: Uses semiprojectivity to obtain actual restriction isomorphisms compatible with the correspondence.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-log-rh)): Over $U$, Riemann–Hilbert identifies $Y_{1,u}$ with the total space over $S$ of this flat bundle at parameter $t(u)$. These identifications are homeomorphisms of families. At $u=0$ they identify the scalar correspondence of Figure <a href="#log:scalar-correspondence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:scalar-correspondence">1</a> at $\lambda=1$ with the flat Betti scalar correspondence, compatibly with its pullbacks and Gysin maps.
  - Explanation: Identifies nonresonant logarithmic families with moving-puncture Betti flat bundles.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-log-scalar-flat-product)): The product identification in Lemma <a href="#log:scalar-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:scalar-product">13</a>, at $\lambda=1$, identifies the scalar unflagged Betti family with $S\times X^{\rm B}$ as a flat bundle with algebraic fiber. Its projection to $X^{\rm B}$ corresponds to $\operatorname{pr}$.
  - Explanation: Makes the scalar unflagged Betti family a flat algebraic product.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-log-finite-ambiguity)): Let a finite group $G$ act algebraically on a smooth connected complex variety $B$, and let $q:B\to B/G$ be the quotient. Suppose $g:A\to B$ is a homeomorphism and $f:A\to B$ is continuous with $qf=qg$. Then $f=\gamma g$ for a single $\gamma\in G$.
  - Explanation: Controls the ambiguity of continuous lifts through a finite quotient.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-log-nah)): The comparison <a href="#log:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="log:comparison">[log:comparison]</a> transports the Deligne weight filtration on the whole $H^*(X^{\rm B},\mathbb Q)$ in the same way as the nonabelian-Hodge identification. The conclusion is unchanged when $\mathcal O_C(d x_0)$ is replaced by an arbitrary $L\in\operatorname{Pic}^d(C)$ and the Higgs spaces are identified by tensoring with a degree-zero nth root of their determinant ratio.
  - Explanation: Checks full-cohomology compatibility with nonabelian Hodge theory for arbitrary determinants.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-trans-translation)): The two modifications define a continuous family of isomorphisms $$\label{trans:path} F_a:Y_{a,v}\longrightarrow Y_{a,v-a\ell},\qquad 0\le a\le1.$$ At $a=0$ they define an algebraic automorphism of $Y_{0,v}$. More generally, the same modification defines an automorphism over the punctured Higgs line $(\lambda,u)=(0,zv)$, $z\ne0$, preserving the logarithmic Hitchin map. At $a=1$, the Riemann–Hilbert identifications make $F_1$ the identity on the underlying Betti space.
  - Explanation: Constructs balanced eigenline modifications and identifies the Betti endpoint.
- **C21** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-log-compact-cycles)): Let $I$ be a connected real interval and let $p:M\to I$ and $q:N\to I$ be smooth submersions, with finite-dimensional rational cohomology on their fibers. Suppose there are spaces $A,B$ and continuous maps $a:M\to A$, $b:N\to B$ whose restrictions induce isomorphisms $$\alpha_t:H^*(A,\mathbb Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^*(M_t,\mathbb Q), \qquad \beta_t:H^*(B,\mathbb Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^*(N_t,\mathbb Q)$$ for every $t\in I$. If $F:M\to N$ is continuous over $I$, then $\alpha_t^{-1}F_t^*\beta_t:H^*(B,\mathbb Q)\to H^*(A,\mathbb Q)$ is independent of $t$.
  - Explanation: Proves constancy of cohomological pullbacks using compact cycles in nonproper submersions.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-trans-logarithm)): There is an integer $M>0$ such that $$\label{trans:N} N=\frac1M\log(\mathsf T^M)$$ is a well-defined nilpotent derivation of $H^*(\mathcal Y,\mathbb Q)$. Let $N_{\lambda,u}=\rho_{\lambda,u}N\rho_{\lambda,u}^{-1}$. On $H^*(Y_{1,0},\mathbb Q)$, equipped with the diagram weights of Section <a href="#sec:diagram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagram">4</a>, one has $$N_{1,0}W_j\subset W_{j-2}.$$
  - Explanation: Transports the finite logarithm to the scalar fiber with its diagram-weight bound.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-trans-weights)): Under nonabelian Hodge theory, the operator <a href="#trans:fprime" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:fprime">[trans:fprime]</a> satisfies $$f'W_jH^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)\subset W_{j-4}H^{m-2}(X^B,\mathbb Q).$$
  - Explanation: Combines the logarithm and flag correspondence to lower Betti weights.
- **C24** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-spec-geometry)): The morphism $g_v$ is proper, and $\mathcal Y_v\to D$ is smooth. The translation defines an automorphism $T_v$ of $\mathcal Y_v\|_{D^*}$ over $\mathcal E\times D^*$. Scaling gives an isomorphism $$\mathcal Y_v\|_{D^*}\simeq Y_{0,v}\times D^*$$ over $D^*$, and restriction from $H^*(\mathcal Y_v)$ to either $Y_{0,0}$ or $Y_{0,v}$ is an isomorphism.
  - Explanation: Places translation over a proper logarithmic Hitchin morphism on the punctured Higgs line.
- **C25** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-spec-specialization)): Let $Y\to D=\mathbb A^1$ be a smooth morphism of complex algebraic varieties, and let $g:Y\to B\times D$ be a proper morphism commuting with the maps to $D$. Suppose that $Y\|_{D^*}$ is isomorphic to $Y_1\times D^*$ over $D^*$ and that the actual restriction maps $$r_i:H^*(Y,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow H^*(Y_i,\mathbb Q), \qquad i=0,1,$$ are isomorphisms. An automorphism $T$ of $Y\|_{D^*}$ over $B\times D^*$ then induces an endomorphism $\tau_0$ of $Rg_{0,*}\mathbb Q_{Y_0}$ whose cohomological action is $$H^*(\tau_0)=r_0r_1^{-1}T_1^*r_1r_0^{-1}.$$ Neither $B$ nor $Y_i$ is required to be proper.
  - Explanation: Realizes transported translation on the special-fiber direct image using nearby cycles.
- **C26** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-spec-perverse-lowering)): The operator $f'$ of <a href="#trans:fprime" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:fprime">[trans:fprime]</a> is induced by a morphism $$\label{spec:derived-lowering} Rh_*\mathbb Q_X\longrightarrow Rh_*\mathbb Q_X[-2]$$ in the constructible derived category on $A$. Consequently $$f'(P_kH^m(X,\mathbb Q))\subseteq P_{k-2}H^{m-2}(X,\mathbb Q)$$ for all integers $m,k$.
  - Explanation: Realizes the lowering operator as a derived morphism over the Hitchin base.
- **C27** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-calc-translation-formula)): For the translation $T:Y_{0,v}\to Y_{0,v}$ associated with $\ell=(1,-1,0,\ldots,0)$ and every integer $k\ge0$, $$\label{calc:translation-polynomial} T^{k*}e_{\mathcal Y}=e_{\mathcal Y}+ky+k^2t_C.$$ Consequently the logarithm from Section <a href="#sec:translation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:translation">6</a> satisfies $$\label{calc:logarithm} N(e_{\mathcal Y})=y$$ on $H^*(\mathcal Y,\mathbb Q)$ and, by restriction, on every fiber.
  - Explanation: Computes translation of the universal characteristic class and its logarithmic derivative.
- **C28** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-calc-flag-sum)): Let $\pi:\operatorname{Fl}(V)\to B$ be the full-flag bundle of a rank-$n$ complex vector bundle, and let $w_1,\ldots,w_n$ be its formal Chern roots. For [(calc:permutation)](#calc:permutation) a polynomial $F$ in the first Chern classes of the ordered flag lines, $$\pi_*\bigl(F(Q_\bullet)c_b(T_\pi)\bigr) =\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak S_n} F(w_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,w_{\sigma(n)}).$$ If $\sum_i\ell_i=0$, then $$\label{calc:permutation} \sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak S_n} \left(\sum_i\ell_iw_{\sigma(i)}\right)^2 =n(n-2)!\\|\ell\\|^2 \left(\sum_iw_i^2-\frac{(\sum_iw_i)^2}{n}\right).$$ Both identities, when their expressions are invariant under common translation of the roots, hold for the associated flag bundle of a principal $\mathrm{PGL}_n$-bundle with rational coefficients.
  - Explanation: Evaluates flag pushforward by permutation sums and a translation-invariant quadratic identity.
- **C29** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-calc-double-commutator)): Let $\delta=\deg(x:S\to C)$. The operator $f'$ of <a href="#trans:fprime" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:fprime">[trans:fprime]</a> satisfies $$\label{calc:scalar} [[f',e],e]=8(-1)^b n(n-2)!\delta\,e.$$ Consequently $$\label{calc:f} f=\frac{(-1)^{b+1}}{4n(n-2)!\delta}\,f'$$ has cohomological degree $-2$, lowers perversity by two and Betti weights by four, and satisfies $[[f,e],e]=-2e$.
  - Explanation: Computes the double commutator and normalizes the common lowering operator.
- **C30** (theorem, [source](#stmt-auto-corollary-1)): Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve of genus $g\ge2$, let $n\ge2$ and $d$ be coprime integers, and let $L\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$. For the moduli space $X$ of stable rank-$n$, determinant-$L$, trace-free Higgs bundles and its corresponding twisted $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n$ character variety $X^B$, nonabelian Hodge theory identifies $$P_k H^m(X,\mathbb Q)=W_{2k}H^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)=W_{2k+1}H^m(X^B,\mathbb Q) \qquad\text{for all integers }m,k.$$ The equality holds on full rational cohomology, including its $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$-variant part.
  - Explanation: Combines composite and established prime-rank cases on full rational cohomology.
- **C31** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-endoscopic-weights)): Keep $C,n,d,L$ as in the preceding corollary. Put $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$, let $\gamma\in\Gamma$ have order $m$, and let $\kappa\in\widehat{\Gamma}$ be the character $\kappa(\gamma')=\langle\gamma,\gamma'\rangle_\Gamma$ defined by the Weil pairing in (Maulik and Shen 2021, sec. 1.3) [[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)]. Let $\pi:C'\to C$ be the associated cyclic étale cover, set $r=n/m$, and put $$d_\gamma=\operatorname{codim}_{\mathcal A^{K_C}}\mathcal A_\gamma^{K_C},$$ where $\mathcal A_\gamma^{K_C}$ is their endoscopic Hitchin base. For $D=K_C$ and $D'=\pi^*K_C=K_{C'}$, let $\mathfrak p_\kappa^B$ denote their nonabelian-Hodge-transported operator in (Maulik and Shen 2021, sec. 5.4) [[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)]. Write $\widetilde{\mathcal M}_{r,d}^B(C')$ for the full degree-$d$ twisted $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_r$ character variety and $\mathcal M_{n,L}^B(C)=X^B$ for the fixed-determinant twisted $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n$ character variety. On complexified cohomology, for all integers $i,k\ge0$, $$\begin{gathered} \mathfrak p_\kappa^B\!\left(W_{2k}H^i(\widetilde{\mathcal M}_{r,d}^B(C'),\mathbb C)\right)\\ =W_{2k+2d_\gamma}H^{i+2d_\gamma}(\mathcal M_{n,L}^B(C),\mathbb C)_\kappa, \end{gathered}$$ where the subscript $\kappa$ denotes the pullback-action summand on which $\gamma'^*\alpha=\kappa(\gamma')\alpha$ for every $\gamma'\in\Gamma$.
  - Explanation: Transfers the exact endoscopic perverse image equality to Betti weights.
- **C32** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-perverse-multiplicativity)): In the setting of the all-rank corollary above, use the normalization <a href="#intro:P" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:P">[intro:P]</a>. For all $i,j\ge0$ and $a,b\in\mathbb Z$, $$P_aH^i(X,\mathbb Q)\smile P_bH^j(X,\mathbb Q) \subseteq P_{a+b}H^{i+j}(X,\mathbb Q).$$ Put $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$ and $P_aH^i(X,\mathbb C)=P_aH^i(X,\mathbb Q)\otimes_{\mathbb Q}\mathbb C$. For the pullback-action isotypic parts indexed by $\chi,\psi\in\widehat\Gamma =\operatorname{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb C^\times)$, one also has $$\bigl(P_aH^i(X,\mathbb C)\bigr)_\chi\smile \bigl(P_bH^j(X,\mathbb C)\bigr)_\psi \subseteq\bigl(P_{a+b}H^{i+j}(X,\mathbb C)\bigr)_{\chi\psi}.$$ This includes trivial and distinct characters, with the same normalized $P$ on every character summand.
  - Explanation: Transfers weight multiplicativity to the full perverse filtration and its character summands.

        "exact_quote": "Let $V,W$ be nonzero finite integer-graded vector spaces over a field of characteristic zero, and let $E_V,E_W$ have degree two.         "anchor": "stmt-betti-geometry",
        "exact_quote": "The morphism $Y^{\\mathrm B}\\to V$ is smooth of relative dimension $2R+2b$. The map $\\iota$ is a regular embedding of codimension $b$ with normal bundle $T_\\pi^*$. The cohomological operators $$\\mathsf A=\\pi_*\\iota^*,\\qquad\n \\mathsf B=\\iota_*\\pi^*$$ have degrees $-2b$ and $2b$, respectively, and satisfy $$\\label{betti:split}\n \\mathsf A\\mathsf B=(-1)^b n!\\,\\mathrm{id}.$$ Here and below a covariant star denotes the Gysin map.",

        "anchor": "stmt-betti-finite-cover",
        "exact_quote": "Pulling the preceding auxiliary bundles and stratifications back along <a href=\"#betti:cover\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"betti:cover\">[betti:cover]</a> has the following consequences.*\n\n1.  *Every fiber of $\\widehat Y^{\\mathrm B}\\to V$ and of $Y^{\\mathrm B}\\to V$ has Hodge–Tate cohomology. For $\\widehat Y^{\\mathrm B}$ at a parameter with distinct eigenvalues, the pulled-back class satisfies curious hard Lefschetz on ordinary cohomology with half-weight center $\\delta/2=R+b+g$.*\n\n2.  *The cohomology sheaves of both ordinary and compact-support integral direct images over $V$ are locally constant and finitely generated. Ordinary direct image commutes with restriction to every fiber. The same assertions hold for $Y^{\\mathrm B}\\to V$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-diag-variation",
        "exact_quote": "Under the preceding hypotheses, $H^j(\\mathscr H_t,\\mathbb Q)$ carries a graded-polarizable mixed Hodge structure, functorial for maps of flat bundles that are algebraic in flat trivializations. As $t$ varies, these structures form an admissible graded-polarizable variation of mixed Hodge structures, with an integral underlying local system up to torsion. They are computed by $H^j(i_t^*K_{\\mathscr H})$.*\n\n*If $H^j(H_t,\\mathbb Q)$ is Hodge–Tate for every $j$ and $t$, then $H^j(\\mathscr H_t,\\mathbb Q)$ is Hodge–Tate as well. We call its weight filtration the *diagram weight filtration*.",
        "source_id": "PM-043.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-diag-gysin",
        "exact_quote": "The diagram weight filtrations have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For the product $S\\times M$, with $M$ a complex algebraic variety, the ordinary Künneth isomorphism identifies its mixed Hodge structure with that on $$H^*(S,\\mathbb Q)\\otimes H^*(M,\\mathbb Q),$$ where every $H^a(S,\\mathbb Q)$ is pure of weight zero and type $(0,0)$, and $H^*(M,\\mathbb Q)$ has its Deligne mixed Hodge structure. If $S$ is oriented and closed of real dimension $d_S$, projection pullback and integration over $S$ preserve weight; the latter has cohomological degree $-d_S$.*\n\n2.  *Pullbacks by maps of flat bundles preserve weight.*\n\n3.  *Suppose $u:\\mathscr H_t\\to\\mathscr H'_t$ is a proper map of flat bundles with smooth equidimensional algebraic fibers. Put $c=\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H'_t-\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H_t$. With the complex orientations on the fibers, its ordinary Gysin map satisfies $$u_*:W_aH^j(\\mathscr H_t)\\longrightarrow\n           W_{a+2c}H^{j+2c}(\\mathscr H'_t).$$ If $S$ is oriented, this is the Gysin map for the induced orientations on the total spaces.",
        "source_id": "PM-043.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-diag-gysin",

If $S$ is oriented and closed of real dimension $d_S$, projection pullback and integration over $S$ preserve weight; the latter has cohomological degree $-d_S$.*\n\n2.  *Pullbacks by maps of flat bundles preserve weight.*\n\n3.  *Suppose $u:\\mathscr H_t\\to\\mathscr H'_t$ is a proper map of flat bundles with smooth equidimensional algebraic fibers. Put $c=\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H'_t-\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H_t$. With the complex orientations on the fibers, its ordinary Gysin map satisfies $$u_*:W_aH^j(\\mathscr H_t)\\longrightarrow\n           W_{a+2c}H^{j+2c}(\\mathscr H'_t).$$ If $S$ is oriented, this is the Gysin map for the induced orientations on the total spaces.",
          "source_id": "PM-043.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "The diagram weight filtrations have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For the product $S\\times M$, with $M$ a complex algebraic variety, the ordinary Künneth isomorphism identifies its mixed Hodge structure with that on $$H^*(S,\\mathbb Q)\\otimes H^*(M,\\mathbb Q),$$ where every $H^a(S,\\mathbb Q)$ is pure of weight zero and type $(0,0)$, and $H^*(M,\\mathbb Q)$ has its Deligne mixed Hodge structure.           "anchor": "stmt-diag-gysin",
          "exact_quote": "The diagram weight filtrations have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For the product $S\\times M$, with $M$ a complex algebraic variety, the ordinary Künneth isomorphism identifies its mixed Hodge structure with that on $$H^*(S,\\mathbb Q)\\otimes H^*(M,\\mathbb Q),$$ where every $H^a(S,\\mathbb Q)$ is pure of weight zero and type $(0,0)$, and $H^*(M,\\mathbb Q)$ has its Deligne mixed Hodge structure. If $S$ is oriented and closed of real dimension $d_S$, projection pullback and integration over $S$ preserve weight; the latter has cohomological degree $-d_S$.*\n\n2.  *Pullbacks by maps of flat bundles preserve weight.*\n\n3.  *Suppose $u:\\mathscr H_t\\to\\mathscr H'_t$ is a proper map of flat bundles with smooth equidimensional algebraic fibers. Put $c=\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H'_t-\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H_t$. With the complex orientations on the fibers, its ordinary Gysin map satisfies $$u_*:W_aH^j(\\mathscr H_t)\\longrightarrow\n           W_{a+2c}H^{j+2c}(\\mathscr H'_t).$$ If $S$ is oriented, this is the Gysin map for the induced orientations on the total spaces.",
          "source_id": "PM-043.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "C11",
      "kind": "conditional_result",
      "quantifiers": [

If $S$ is oriented and closed of real dimension $d_S$, projection pullback and integration over $S$ preserve weight; the latter has cohomological degree $-d_S$.*\n\n2.  *Pullbacks by maps of flat bundles preserve weight.*\n\n3.  *Suppose $u:\\mathscr H_t\\to\\mathscr H'_t$ is a proper map of flat bundles with smooth equidimensional algebraic fibers. Put $c=\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H'_t-\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H_t$.           "source_id": "PM-043.1"
        }

        "anchor": "stmt-diag-finite",
        "exact_quote": "Assume the Hodge–Tate hypothesis of Proposition <a href=\"#diag:variation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"diag:variation\">10</a>. The combined monodromy action on all $\\operatorname{Gr}^W_aH^j(\\mathscr H_t,\\mathbb Q)$ has finite image. For any loop with cohomology monodromy $T$, there is an integer $M>0$ such that $T^M$ is unipotent and $$\\label{diag:log}\n  N=\\frac{1}{M}\\log(T^M)\n  \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad N(W_a)\\subset W_{a-2}.$$ The logarithm is a finite polynomial. On the total cohomology ring, $N$ is a derivation of cohomological degree zero.",

        "anchor": "stmt-log-families",
        "exact_quote": "The morphism $\\mathcal Y\\to S\\times\\Lambda$ is smooth of relative dimension $2R+2b$, and $\\mathcal X\\to\\mathbb A^1$ is smooth of relative dimension $2R$. The same holds for the inverse image in $\\mathcal Y$ of every linear subspace of $\\Lambda$ through zero. In Figure <a href=\"#log:scalar-correspondence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"log:scalar-correspondence\">1</a>, $\\pi$ is a smooth proper flag projection of relative dimension $b$, and $\\iota$ is a regular embedding of codimension $b$ with normal bundle $T_\\pi^*$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-trans-translation",
        "exact_quote": "The two modifications define a continuous family of isomorphisms $$\\label{trans:path}\nF_a:Y_{a,v}\\longrightarrow Y_{a,v-a\\ell},\\qquad 0\\le a\\le1.$$ At $a=0$ they define an algebraic automorphism of $Y_{0,v}$. More generally, the same modification defines an automorphism over the punctured Higgs line $(\\lambda,u)=(0,zv)$, $z\\ne0$, preserving the logarithmic Hitchin map. At $a=1$, the Riemann–Hilbert identifications make $F_1$ the identity on the underlying Betti space.",
        "source_id": "PM-043.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-log-compact-cycles",
        "exact_quote": "Let $I$ be a connected real interval and let $p:M\\to I$ and $q:N\\to I$ be smooth submersions, with finite-dimensional rational cohomology on their fibers. Suppose there are spaces $A,B$ and continuous maps $a:M\\to A$, $b:N\\to B$ whose restrictions induce isomorphisms $$\\alpha_t:H^*(A,\\mathbb Q)\\xrightarrow{\\sim}H^*(M_t,\\mathbb Q),\n \\qquad\n \\beta_t:H^*(B,\\mathbb Q)\\xrightarrow{\\sim}H^*(N_t,\\mathbb Q)$$ for every $t\\in I$. If $F:M\\to N$ is continuous over $I$, then $\\alpha_t^{-1}F_t^*\\beta_t:H^*(B,\\mathbb Q)\\to H^*(A,\\mathbb Q)$ is independent of $t$.",
        "source_id": "PM-043.1"
      },

If $F:M\\to N$ is continuous over $I$, then $\\alpha_t^{-1}F_t^*\\beta_t:H^*(B,\\mathbb Q)\\to H^*(A,\\mathbb Q)$ is independent of $t$.",
          "source_id": "PM-043.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-spec-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y\\to D=\\mathbb A^1$ be a smooth morphism of complex algebraic varieties, and let $g:Y\\to B\\times D$ be a proper morphism commuting with the maps to $D$.         "exact_quote": "For the translation $T:Y_{0,v}\\to Y_{0,v}$ associated with $\\ell=(1,-1,0,\\ldots,0)$ and every integer $k\\ge0$, $$\\label{calc:translation-polynomial}\n T^{k*}e_{\\mathcal Y}=e_{\\mathcal Y}+ky+k^2t_C.$$ Consequently the logarithm from Section <a href=\"#sec:translation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"sec:translation\">6</a> satisfies $$\\label{calc:logarithm}\n N(e_{\\mathcal Y})=y$$ on $H^*(\\mathcal Y,\\mathbb Q)$ and, by restriction, on every fiber.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-calc-flag-sum",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\pi:\\operatorname{Fl}(V)\\to B$ be the full-flag bundle of a rank-$n$ complex vector bundle, and let $w_1,\\ldots,w_n$ be its formal Chern roots. For [(calc:permutation)](#calc:permutation) a polynomial $F$ in the first Chern classes of the ordered flag lines, $$\\pi_*\\bigl(F(Q_\\bullet)c_b(T_\\pi)\\bigr)\n   =\\sum_{\\sigma\\in\\mathfrak S_n}\n      F(w_{\\sigma(1)},\\ldots,w_{\\sigma(n)}).$$ If $\\sum_i\\ell_i=0$, then $$\\label{calc:permutation}\n \\sum_{\\sigma\\in\\mathfrak S_n}\n       \\left(\\sum_i\\ell_iw_{\\sigma(i)}\\right)^2\n =n(n-2)!\\|\\ell\\|^2\n    \\left(\\sum_iw_i^2-\\frac{(\\sum_iw_i)^2}{n}\\right).$$ Both identities, when their expressions are invariant under common translation of the roots, hold for the associated flag bundle of a principal $\\mathrm{PGL}_n$-bundle with rational coefficients.",
        "source_id": "PM-043.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-calc-flag-sum",

          "source_id": "PM-043.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-auto-corollary-1",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve of genus $g\\ge2$, let $n\\ge2$ and $d$ be coprime integers, and let $L\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$. For the moduli space $X$ of stable rank-$n$, determinant-$L$, trace-free Higgs bundles and its corresponding twisted $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_n$ character variety $X^B$, nonabelian Hodge theory identifies $$P_k H^m(X,\\mathbb Q)=W_{2k}H^m(X^B,\\mathbb Q)=W_{2k+1}H^m(X^B,\\mathbb Q)\n \\qquad\\text{for all integers }m,k.$$ The equality holds on full rational cohomology, including its $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$-variant part.",
        "source_id": "PM-043.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-auto-corollary-1",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve of genus $g\\ge2$, let $n\\ge2$ and $d$ be coprime integers, and let $L\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$. For the moduli space $X$ of stable rank-$n$, determinant-$L$, trace-free Higgs bundles and its corresponding twisted $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_n$ character variety $X^B$, nonabelian Hodge theory identifies $$P_k H^m(X,\\mathbb Q)=W_{2k}H^m(X^B,\\mathbb Q)=W_{2k+1}H^m(X^B,\\mathbb Q)\n \\qquad\\text{for all integers }m,k.$$ The equality holds on full rational cohomology, including its $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$-variant part.",
          "source_id": "PM-043.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve of genus $g\\ge2$, let $n\\ge2$ and $d$ be coprime integers, and let $L\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$.       "explanation": "Combines composite and established prime-rank cases on full rational cohomology.",

      "hypotheses": [

          "anchor": "stmt-auto-corollary-1",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-endoscopic-weights",
        "exact_quote": "Keep $C,n,d,L$ as in the preceding corollary. Put $\\Gamma=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$, let $\\gamma\\in\\Gamma$ have order $m$, and let $\\kappa\\in\\widehat{\\Gamma}$ be the character $\\kappa(\\gamma')=\\langle\\gamma,\\gamma'\\rangle_\\Gamma$ defined by the Weil pairing in (Maulik and Shen 2021, sec. 1.3) [[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)]. Let $\\pi:C'\\to C$ be the associated cyclic étale cover, set $r=n/m$, and put $$d_\\gamma=\\operatorname{codim}_{\\mathcal A^{K_C}}\\mathcal A_\\gamma^{K_C},$$ where $\\mathcal A_\\gamma^{K_C}$ is their endoscopic Hitchin base. For $D=K_C$ and $D'=\\pi^*K_C=K_{C'}$, let $\\mathfrak p_\\kappa^B$ denote their nonabelian-Hodge-transported operator in (Maulik and Shen 2021, sec. 5.4) [[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)]. Write $\\widetilde{\\mathcal M}_{r,d}^B(C')$ for the full degree-$d$ twisted $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_r$ character variety and $\\mathcal M_{n,L}^B(C)=X^B$ for the fixed-determinant twisted $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_n$ character variety. On complexified cohomology, for all integers $i,k\\ge0$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n\\mathfrak p_\\kappa^B\\!\\left(W_{2k}H^i(\\widetilde{\\mathcal M}_{r,d}^B(C'),\\mathbb C)\\right)\\\\\n=W_{2k+2d_\\gamma}H^{i+2d_\\gamma}(\\mathcal M_{n,L}^B(C),\\mathbb C)_\\kappa,\n\\end{gathered}$$ where the subscript $\\kappa$ denotes the pullback-action summand on which $\\gamma'^*\\alpha=\\kappa(\\gamma')\\alpha$ for every $\\gamma'\\in\\Gamma$.",
        "source_id": "PM-043.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

For $D=K_C$ and $D'=\\pi^*K_C=K_{C'}$, let $\\mathfrak p_\\kappa^B$ denote their nonabelian-Hodge-transported operator in (Maulik and Shen 2021, sec. 5.4) [[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)]. Write $\\widetilde{\\mathcal M}_{r,d}^B(C')$ for the full degree-$d$ twisted $\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}_r$ character variety and $\\mathcal M_{n,L}^B(C)=X^B$ for the fixed-determinant twisted $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SL}}_n$ character variety. On complexified cohomology, for all integers $i,k\\ge0$, $$\\begin{gathered}\n\\mathfrak p_\\kappa^B\\!\\left(W_{2k}H^i(\\widetilde{\\mathcal M}_{r,d}^B(C'),\\mathbb C)\\right)\\\\\n=W_{2k+2d_\\gamma}H^{i+2d_\\gamma}(\\mathcal M_{n,L}^B(C),\\mathbb C)_\\kappa,\n\\end{gathered}$$ where the subscript $\\kappa$ denotes the pullback-action summand on which $\\gamma'^*\\alpha=\\kappa(\\gamma')\\alpha$ for every $\\gamma'\\in\\Gamma$.",
          "source_id": "PM-043.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-cor-endoscopic-weights"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-perverse-multiplicativity",
        "exact_quote": "In the setting of the all-rank corollary above, use the normalization <a href=\"#intro:P\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"intro:P\">[intro:P]</a>. For all $i,j\\ge0$ and $a,b\\in\\mathbb Z$, $$P_aH^i(X,\\mathbb Q)\\smile P_bH^j(X,\\mathbb Q)\n\\subseteq P_{a+b}H^{i+j}(X,\\mathbb Q).$$ Put $\\Gamma=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$ and $P_aH^i(X,\\mathbb C)=P_aH^i(X,\\mathbb Q)\\otimes_{\\mathbb Q}\\mathbb C$. For the pullback-action isotypic parts indexed by $\\chi,\\psi\\in\\widehat\\Gamma\n=\\operatorname{Hom}(\\Gamma,\\mathbb C^\\times)$, one also has $$\\bigl(P_aH^i(X,\\mathbb C)\\bigr)_\\chi\\smile\n\\bigl(P_bH^j(X,\\mathbb C)\\bigr)_\\psi\n\\subseteq\\bigl(P_{a+b}H^{i+j}(X,\\mathbb C)\\bigr)_{\\chi\\psi}.$$ This includes trivial and distinct characters, with the same normalized $P$ on every character summand.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Fixed-determinant trace-free Higgs moduli** ([source](#intro:betti)): Write $$X=M_{\mathrm{Dol}}^L(\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n)$$ for the moduli space of stable rank-$n$ Higgs bundles $(E,\theta)$ with $\det E\simeq L$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\theta=0$.
- **Stability** ([source](#intro:betti)): Here stability means that every proper nonzero $\theta$-invariant subbundle has slope smaller than $d/n$.
- **Hitchin map** ([source](#intro:betti)): Its Hitchin map is $$h:X\longrightarrow A=\bigoplus_{i=2}^nH^0(C,K_C^i),$$ given by the coefficients of the characteristic polynomial.
- **Perverse filtration** ([source](#intro:P)): Thus the standard $P=W$ normalization is $$\label{intro:P} P_kH^m(X)=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\left\{ H^{m-R}\!\left(A,{}^p\tau_{\le k}Rh_*\mathbb Q_X[R]\right) \longrightarrow H^m(X)\right\}.$$
- **Diagram complex** ([source](#diag:complex)): Define $$\label{diag:complex} K_{\mathscr H} =\mathop{\mathrm{holim}}_I Rp_{I*}\mathbb Q_{H_I}^H \quad\text{in }D^b\mathrm{MHM}(V).$$
- **Unnormalized lowering operator** ([source](#trans:fprime)): We define $$\label{trans:fprime} f'=\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}_{0,*}\,\mathsf A_0 N_{0,0}^2\mathsf B_0\,\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}_0^* :H^m(X,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow H^{m-2}(X,\mathbb Q).$$
- **Flag difference and tangent classes** ([source](#pm-h-31)): We use the global classes $$y=c_1(Q_1\otimes Q_2^{-1}),\qquad t_C=x^*c_1(T_C),$$ where the second is pulled back from $S$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **S02** [claim](#stmt-norm-dimensions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-dimensions): Establishes the dimensions and equidimensionality needed to normalize the Hitchin filtration. -> Establishes the dimensions and equidimensionality needed to normalize the Hitchin filtration.
- **S03** [claim](#stmt-norm-low-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-low-cohomology): Identifies the unique degree-two raiser and rules out low-degree variant contributions. -> Identifies the unique degree-two raiser and rules out low-degree variant contributions.
- **S04** [claim](#stmt-norm-relative-lefschetz) / [proof](#proof-stmt-norm-relative-lefschetz): Supplies relative hard Lefschetz for the normalized perverse filtration. -> Supplies relative hard Lefschetz for the normalized perverse filtration.
- **S05** [claim](#stmt-alg-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-comparison): Determines both filtrations from the same raiser and lowering operator, including extensions. -> Determines both filtrations from the same raiser and lowering operator, including extensions.
- **S06** [claim](#stmt-alg-tensor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-alg-tensor): Cancels auxiliary tensor factors from a Lefschetz symmetry. -> Cancels auxiliary tensor factors from a Lefschetz symmetry.
- **S08** [claim](#stmt-betti-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-betti-geometry): Constructs a split flag correspondence with controlled degree shifts. -> Constructs a split flag correspondence with controlled degree shifts.
- **S09** [claim](#stmt-betti-finite-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-betti-finite-cover): Retains all lifted strata and proves ordinary base change without assuming properness of the family. -> Retains all lifted strata and proves ordinary base change without assuming properness of the family.
- **S10** [claim](#stmt-diag-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-diag-variation): Builds diagram mixed Hodge structures and their variations from algebraic fiber diagrams. -> Builds diagram mixed Hodge structures and their variations from algebraic fiber diagrams.
- **S11** [claim](#stmt-diag-gysin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-diag-gysin): Separates weight-preserving base integration from the Tate shifts of fiber Gysin maps. -> Separates weight-preserving base integration from the Tate shifts of fiber Gysin maps.
- **S12** [claim](#stmt-diag-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-diag-finite): Uses integral lattices and polarizations to obtain a weight-lowering monodromy logarithm. -> Uses integral lattices and polarizations to obtain a weight-lowering monodromy logarithm.
- **S07** [claim](#stmt-betti-lefschetz) / [proof](#sec:betti): Supplies curious hard Lefschetz and even weights on full Betti cohomology. -> Supplies curious hard Lefschetz and even weights on full Betti cohomology.
- **S13** [claim](#stmt-log-scalar-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-log-scalar-product): Corrects the moving determinant by tensoring and identifies the scalar family as a product. -> Corrects the moving determinant by tensoring and identifies the scalar family as a product.
- **S14** [claim](#stmt-log-families) / [proof](#proof-stmt-log-families): Checks deformation smoothness, scaling limits, and the scalar embedding normal bundle. -> Checks deformation smoothness, scaling limits, and the scalar embedding normal bundle.
- **S15** [claim](#stmt-log-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-log-restriction): Uses semiprojectivity to obtain actual restriction isomorphisms compatible with the correspondence. -> Uses semiprojectivity to obtain actual restriction isomorphisms compatible with the correspondence.
- **S16** [claim](#stmt-log-rh) / [proof](#proof-stmt-log-rh): Identifies nonresonant logarithmic families with moving-puncture Betti flat bundles. -> Identifies nonresonant logarithmic families with moving-puncture Betti flat bundles.
- **S17** [claim](#stmt-log-scalar-flat-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-log-scalar-flat-product): Makes the scalar unflagged Betti family a flat algebraic product. -> Makes the scalar unflagged Betti family a flat algebraic product.
- **S18** [claim](#stmt-log-finite-ambiguity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-log-finite-ambiguity): Controls the ambiguity of continuous lifts through a finite quotient. -> Controls the ambiguity of continuous lifts through a finite quotient.
- **S19** [claim](#stmt-log-nah) / [proof](#proof-stmt-log-nah): Checks full-cohomology compatibility with nonabelian Hodge theory for arbitrary determinants. -> Checks full-cohomology compatibility with nonabelian Hodge theory for arbitrary determinants.
- **S20** [claim](#stmt-trans-translation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-translation): Constructs balanced eigenline modifications and identifies the Betti endpoint. -> Constructs balanced eigenline modifications and identifies the Betti endpoint.
- **S21** [claim](#stmt-log-compact-cycles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-log-compact-cycles): Proves constancy of cohomological pullbacks using compact cycles in nonproper submersions. -> Proves constancy of cohomological pullbacks using compact cycles in nonproper submersions.
- **S22** [claim](#stmt-trans-logarithm) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-logarithm): Transports the finite logarithm to the scalar fiber with its diagram-weight bound. -> Transports the finite logarithm to the scalar fiber with its diagram-weight bound.
- **S23** [claim](#stmt-trans-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-trans-weights): Combines the logarithm and flag correspondence to lower Betti weights. -> Combines the logarithm and flag correspondence to lower Betti weights.
- **S24** [claim](#stmt-spec-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-geometry): Places translation over a proper logarithmic Hitchin morphism on the punctured Higgs line. -> Places translation over a proper logarithmic Hitchin morphism on the punctured Higgs line.
- **S25** [claim](#stmt-spec-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-specialization): Realizes transported translation on the special-fiber direct image using nearby cycles. -> Realizes transported translation on the special-fiber direct image using nearby cycles.
- **S26** [claim](#stmt-spec-perverse-lowering) / [proof](#proof-stmt-spec-perverse-lowering): Realizes the lowering operator as a derived morphism over the Hitchin base. -> Realizes the lowering operator as a derived morphism over the Hitchin base.
- **S27** [claim](#stmt-calc-translation-formula) / [proof](#proof-stmt-calc-translation-formula): Computes translation of the universal characteristic class and its logarithmic derivative. -> Computes translation of the universal characteristic class and its logarithmic derivative.
- **S28** [claim](#stmt-calc-flag-sum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-calc-flag-sum): Evaluates flag pushforward by permutation sums and a translation-invariant quadratic identity. -> Evaluates flag pushforward by permutation sums and a translation-invariant quadratic identity.
- **S29** [claim](#stmt-calc-double-commutator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-calc-double-commutator): Computes the double commutator and normalizes the common lowering operator. -> Computes the double commutator and normalizes the common lowering operator.
- **S01** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-calc-double-commutator-2): Identifies the perverse and weight filtrations on full cohomology in composite coprime rank. -> Identifies the perverse and weight filtrations on full cohomology in composite coprime rank.
- **S30** [claim](#stmt-auto-corollary-1) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-corollary-1): Combines composite and established prime-rank cases on full rational cohomology. -> Combines composite and established prime-rank cases on full rational cohomology.
- **S31** [claim](#stmt-cor-endoscopic-weights) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-endoscopic-weights): Transfers the exact endoscopic perverse image equality to Betti weights. -> Transfers the exact endoscopic perverse image equality to Betti weights.
- **S32** [claim](#stmt-cor-perverse-multiplicativity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-perverse-multiplicativity): Transfers weight multiplicativity to the full perverse filtration and its character summands. -> Transfers weight multiplicativity to the full perverse filtration and its character summands.

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-diag-variation",
          "exact_quote": "Under the preceding hypotheses, $H^j(\\mathscr H_t,\\mathbb Q)$ carries a graded-polarizable mixed Hodge structure, functorial for maps of flat bundles that are algebraic in flat trivializations. As $t$ varies, these structures form an admissible graded-polarizable variation of mixed Hodge structures, with an integral underlying local system up to torsion. They are computed by $H^j(i_t^*K_{\\mathscr H})$.*\n\n*If $H^j(H_t,\\mathbb Q)$ is Hodge–Tate for every $j$ and $t$, then $H^j(\\mathscr H_t,\\mathbb Q)$ is Hodge–Tate as well. We call its weight filtration the *diagram weight filtration*.",
          "source_id": "PM-043.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "S10",
      "inputs": [
        "The hypotheses in the cited source statement."

      "method": "Builds diagram mixed Hodge structures and their variations from algebraic fiber diagrams.",
      "output": "Builds diagram mixed Hodge structures and their variations from algebraic fiber diagrams.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-diag-gysin",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-diag-gysin",
          "exact_quote": "The diagram weight filtrations have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *For the product $S\\times M$, with $M$ a complex algebraic variety, the ordinary Künneth isomorphism identifies its mixed Hodge structure with that on $$H^*(S,\\mathbb Q)\\otimes H^*(M,\\mathbb Q),$$ where every $H^a(S,\\mathbb Q)$ is pure of weight zero and type $(0,0)$, and $H^*(M,\\mathbb Q)$ has its Deligne mixed Hodge structure. If $S$ is oriented and closed of real dimension $d_S$, projection pullback and integration over $S$ preserve weight; the latter has cohomological degree $-d_S$.*\n\n2.  *Pullbacks by maps of flat bundles preserve weight.*\n\n3.  *Suppose $u:\\mathscr H_t\\to\\mathscr H'_t$ is a proper map of flat bundles with smooth equidimensional algebraic fibers. Put $c=\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H'_t-\\dim_{\\mathbb C}H_t$. With the complex orientations on the fibers, its ordinary Gysin map satisfies $$u_*:W_aH^j(\\mathscr H_t)\\longrightarrow\n           W_{a+2c}H^{j+2c}(\\mathscr H'_t).$$ If $S$ is oriented, this is the Gysin map for the induced orientations on the total spaces.",
          "source_id": "PM-043.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "S11",
      "inputs": [
        "The hypotheses in the cited source statement."
      ],
      "method": "Separates weight-preserving base integration from the Tate shifts of fiber Gysin maps.",
      "output": "Separates weight-preserving base integration from the Tate shifts of fiber Gysin maps.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-diag-gysin"
    },

For any loop with cohomology monodromy $T$, there is an integer $M>0$ such that $T^M$ is unipotent and $$\\label{diag:log}\n  N=\\frac{1}{M}\\log(T^M)\n  \\quad\\text{satisfies}\\quad N(W_a)\\subset W_{a-2}.$$ The logarithm is a finite polynomial. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-permutation**: The quadratic permutation sum equals the centered quadratic root expression multiplied by the coefficient norm and the stated factorial factor.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-nonzero-sum**: Apply the centered-root identity to an arbitrary coefficient vector. -> The reduction of the cross terms requires the coefficient sum to vanish.
- **A-full-theorem**: Treat agreement of the finite permutation calculation as verification of the filtration comparison theorem. -> The geometric hypotheses and cohomological constructions are outside this finite calculation.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The permutation identity is a finite sum with an explicit exact expression; its arithmetic specialization can be checked independently.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of cases, each with equally long rational-string ell and roots arrays.
# PRECONDITIONS: lengths n from 2 through 6; sum(ell)=0; rational exact inputs.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of canonical rational strings for the quadratic permutation sums.
# TIME: O(cases*n) rational operations, plus factorial coefficient evaluation.
# SPACE: O(cases*n) input storage and O(cases) output storage.
# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpq and fmpz; no floating point.
# FAILURE: malformed, out-of-range, or nonzero-sum input raises ValueError.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq, fmpz
answers = []
for record in json.load(sys.stdin):
    weights = [fmpq(s) for s in record['ell']]
    roots = [fmpq(s) for s in record['roots']]
    n = len(weights)
    if not 2 <= n <= 6 or len(roots) != n or sum(weights) != 0:
        raise ValueError('precondition')
    norm = sum(a*a for a in weights)
    centered = sum(a*a for a in roots) - sum(roots)**2 / n
    coefficient = n * fmpz.fac_ui(n-2)
    answer = coefficient * norm * centered
    answers.append(str(answer))
print(json.dumps(answers))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Exact symbolic cohomology software can use the common lowering-operator criterion as a conditional consistency contract for supplied finite-dimensional filtration data. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the paper provides no game dynamics or simulation model; its filtration identities concern cohomology. |
| tools | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A symbolic verification tool can compare the finite permutation sum with its trace-free quadratic expression and detect algebraic normalization errors. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite permutation formula supports an exact evaluation kernel for supplied roots and cocharacter entries. The comparison proof also gives an eigenspace reconstruction rule under its stated hypotheses. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The proven eigenspace decomposition can organize a finite filtration as graded subspaces instead of separate nested subspace descriptions. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no hardware architecture, accelerator mapping or execution benchmark is supplied. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the finite group and character actions are geometric cohomology constructions, with no security model. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no learning objective, training procedure or statistical prediction guarantee is supplied. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: no optimisation objective, feasible-region algorithm or operations-research contract is supplied. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT: the locally constant transition diagrams concern algebraic fibers and cohomology, with no distributed execution or consistency protocol. |
| other (Algebraic geometry and mixed Hodge theory) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The principal application is a mathematical compatibility contract between the perverse and weight filtrations on full rational cohomology in the smooth coprime fixed-determinant, trace-free setting. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (hypothesis): The main theorem requires a smooth projective complex curve of genus $g\ge2$, composite rank $n\ge4$, coprime degree, fixed determinant and trace-free Higgs bundles. Blocked use: ['Applying the composite-rank theorem to singular or nonprojective curves, lower genus, noncoprime degree, or unrestricted determinant and trace.']
- **L02** (edge_case): The all-rank conclusion incorporates established prime-rank cases; the composite-rank theorem alone is not its entire proof. Blocked use: ['Attributing the prime-rank cases to the composite-rank construction alone.']
- **L03** (formal_scope): The filtration equality is stated for rational cohomology, including the variant part. It does not supply an integral or torsion comparison. Blocked use: ['Using the equality as an integral-cohomology or torsion certificate.']
- **L04** (hypothesis): The algebraic comparison requires finite-dimensional characteristic-zero data with finite, exhaustive, separated increasing integer-indexed filtrations. Blocked use: ['Applying the comparison to infinite-dimensional data, positive characteristic, or filtrations missing these properties.']
- **L05** (hypothesis): A common lowering operator, the same Lefschetz center and the double-commutator identity are prerequisites for the filtration comparison. Blocked use: ['Concluding filtration equality from matching dimensions, a shared raiser, or a commutator identity alone.']
- **L06** (hypothesis): The tensor cancellation statement requires nonzero finite integer-graded factors over characteristic zero and degree-two operators. Blocked use: ['Cancelling a zero factor or applying the conclusion to arbitrary ungraded operators.']
- **L07** (hypothesis): Smoothness alone does not establish ordinary base change for a nonproper morphism; the locally constant integral direct images and natural fiber maps are additional hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Replacing ordinary base-change hypotheses by nonproper smoothness alone.']
- **L08** (edge_case): Diagram weights give the moving-point cochains weight zero and differ from ordinary Deligne weights on an algebraic moving-point curve. Blocked use: ['Assigning the ordinary curve weights while retaining the claimed weight-preserving integration.']
- **L09** (hypothesis): The Riemann–Hilbert family comparison is restricted to the prescribed nonresonant exponent domain, with zero differences allowed. Blocked use: ['Extending the comparison to nonzero integral resonance without another argument.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): Specialization requires a smooth family, a proper morphism over the base, the punctured product and actual cohomology restriction isomorphisms. Blocked use: ['Commuting nearby cycles with a nonproper direct image to a point solely from smoothness.']
- **L11** (edge_case): The translation logarithm does not annihilate the flag difference class; its derivative includes the tangent-class term. Blocked use: ['Dropping the quadratic translation term by assuming the flag difference class has zero logarithmic derivative.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The finite quadratic permutation identity assumes that the cocharacter entries sum to zero. Blocked use: ['Using the centered quadratic formula for an arbitrary cocharacter.']
- **L13** (nonconstructive): Finite exact permutation evaluation checks the quadratic flag identity. It does not compute the full moduli-space cohomology or certify the geometric filtration equality. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite permutation check as verification of the full geometric theorem.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the <i>P</i> = <i>W</i> conjecture on the full rational cohomology of fixed-determinant, trace-free Higgs moduli spaces in composite rank and coprime degree, for smooth projective complex curves of genus at least two. Together with the established prime-rank cases, this gives the equality in every coprime rank.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | For every smooth projective complex curve $C$ of genus $g\ge2$, every composite integer $n\ge4$, every integer $d$ with $\gcd(n,d)=1$, and every $L\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$, one has |

<a id="pm-h-2"></a>
### Introduction

Nonabelian Hodge theory identifies two moduli spaces whose cohomology carries very different geometric filtrations. On the Higgs-bundle side, the Hitchin map defines the perverse Leray filtration. On the character-variety side, the mixed Hodge structure defines the weight filtration. The $P=W$ conjecture asserts that these filtrations coincide after doubling the indices of the first.

The geometric setting comes from Hitchin’s moduli spaces and integrable systems (N. J. Hitchin 1987; N. Hitchin 1987) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Hitchin1987SelfDuality](#ref-Hitchin1987SelfDuality), [Hitchin1987Integrable](#ref-Hitchin1987Integrable)]. The analytic correspondence rests on the foundational work of Hitchin, Donaldson, Corlette, and Simpson (N. J. Hitchin 1987; Donaldson 1987; Corlette 1988; C. T. Simpson 1992) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Hitchin1987SelfDuality](#ref-Hitchin1987SelfDuality), [Donaldson1987](#ref-Donaldson1987), [Corlette1988](#ref-Corlette1988), [Simpson1992](#ref-Simpson1992)]. We use its twisted, fixed-determinant form, including the action by torsion line bundles, as described by Hausel and Thaddeus (Hausel and Thaddeus 2003, sec. 2 and 5) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[HT2003](#ref-HT2003)].

<a id="intro:betti"></a>
Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve of genus $g\ge2$. Fix an integer $n\ge2$, an integer $d$ coprime to $n$, and a line bundle $L\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$. Write $$X=M_{\mathrm{Dol}}^L(\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n)$$ for the moduli space of stable rank-$n$ Higgs bundles $(E,\theta)$ with $\det E\simeq L$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\theta=0$. Here stability means that every proper nonzero $\theta$-invariant subbundle has slope smaller than $d/n$. Its Hitchin map is $$h:X\longrightarrow A=\bigoplus_{i=2}^nH^0(C,K_C^i),$$ given by the coefficients of the characteristic polynomial. Put $$R=(n^2-1)(g-1),\qquad b=\frac{n(n-1)}2,
\qquad \zeta=\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}d/n).$$ The corresponding twisted character variety is $$\label{intro:betti}
X^B=\left\{(A_1,B_1,\ldots,A_g,B_g)\in\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n(\mathbb C)^{2g}:
\prod_{j=1}^g[A_j,B_j]=\zeta I_n\right\}\mathbin{/\mkern-6mu/}\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n(\mathbb C),$$ where the group acts by simultaneous conjugation and $[A,B]=ABA^{-1}B^{-1}$. Coprimality makes both spaces smooth and makes every representation in <a href="#intro:betti" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:betti">[intro:betti]</a> irreducible. Nonabelian Hodge theory gives a diffeomorphism between them (Hausel and Thaddeus 2003; C. Simpson 1997) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HT2003](#ref-HT2003), [Simpson1997](#ref-Simpson1997)].

<a id="intro:P"></a>
We use rational cohomology unless complex coefficients are indicated. The Hitchin map is proper, $\dim X=2R$, $\dim A=R$, and $$\dim(X\times_A X)-\dim X=R.$$ Thus the standard $P=W$ normalization is $$\label{intro:P}
P_kH^m(X)=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\left\{
H^{m-R}\!\left(A,{}^p\tau_{\le k}Rh_*\mathbb Q_X[R]\right)
\longrightarrow H^m(X)\right\}.$$ Here ${}^p\tau$ denotes truncation for the middle perverse $t$-structure. We recall the dimension and shift conventions in Section <a href="#sec:lefschetz" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lefschetz">2</a>. Transport $P$ to $H^*(X^B)$ by nonabelian Hodge theory, and let $W$ denote Deligne’s weight filtration there (Deligne 1971, 1974) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[Deligne1971](#ref-Deligne1971), [Deligne1974](#ref-Deligne1974)].

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *For every smooth projective complex curve $C$ of genus $g\ge2$, every composite integer $n\ge4$, every integer $d$ with $\gcd(n,d)=1$, and every $L\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$, one has $$P_kH^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)=W_{2k}H^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)
=W_{2k+1}H^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)$$ for all $m,k\ge0$.*

</div>

The assertion concerns the entire cohomology. The group $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$ acts on $X$ by tensor product. Its averaging projector $$\operatorname{av}_{\Gamma}=\frac1{|\Gamma|}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma}\gamma^*$$ has image $H^*(X,\mathbb Q)^\Gamma$ and kernel the *variant cohomology* $H^*_{\mathrm{var}}(X,\mathbb Q)$. Thus, after complexification, the theorem includes every nontrivial $\Gamma$-character, not only the invariant part. Combining Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> with the established prime-rank cases (Maulik and Shen 2024, Theorem 0.3) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[MS2024](#ref-MS2024)] resolves the smooth coprime fixed-determinant, trace-free $P=W$ conjecture in every rank $n\ge2$.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and the fixed-determinant problem

The weight symmetries behind $P=W$ arose from the arithmetic study of character varieties by Hausel and Rodriguez-Villegas (Hausel and Rodriguez-Villegas 2008) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[HRV2008](#ref-HRV2008)] <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[HLRV2011](#ref-HLRV2011)], extended to generic puncture data by Hausel, Letellier, and Rodriguez-Villegas (Hausel, Letellier, and Rodriguez-Villegas 2011). De Cataldo, Hausel, and Migliorini formulated the filtration comparison and established it in rank two (M. A. A. de Cataldo, Hausel, and Migliorini 2012) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[dCHM2012](#ref-dCHM2012)]. Their theorem relates the topology of an algebraic integrable system to the mixed Hodge theory of an affine character variety: relative hard Lefschetz on the Higgs side explains curious hard Lefschetz on the Betti side.

Tautological classes have been central to the higher-rank problem. For $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n$, generation by these classes was established in rank two by Hausel and Thaddeus (Hausel and Thaddeus 2004) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[HTGenerators2004](#ref-HTGenerators2004)] and in arbitrary rank by Markman (Markman 2002, Theorem 7) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Markman2002](#ref-Markman2002)]; Shende determined their Betti weights (Shende 2017) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Shende2017](#ref-Shende2017)]. De Cataldo, Maulik, and Shen proved $P=W$ for $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n$ in genus two and obtained the required perversity bounds for tautological classes in arbitrary genus (M. A. de Cataldo, Maulik, and Shen 2022, Theorems 0.2 and 0.4–0.6) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[dCMSJAMS2022](#ref-dCMSJAMS2022)].

Maulik and Shen proved the coprime $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_n$ conjecture in arbitrary rank and genus (Maulik and Shen 2024) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[MS2024](#ref-MS2024)]. Further proofs use the Hecke-algebra action of Hausel, Mellit, Minets, and Schiffmann (Hausel et al. 2025) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[HMMS2025](#ref-HMMS2025)] and the Fourier-transform method of Maulik, Shen, and Yin (Maulik, Shen, and Yin 2025, Theorem 0.4) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[MSY2025](#ref-MSY2025)].

For fixed determinant, the quotient by $\Gamma$ detects the invariant cohomology, and the projective tautological generation theorem applies to this quotient (Maulik and Shen 2024, Theorem 2.1(i)) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[MS2024](#ref-MS2024)]. It does not generate the variant part. De Cataldo, Maulik, and Shen determined the two filtrations on the variant part in prime rank and reduced $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_p$ to $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_p$ (M. A. A. de Cataldo, Maulik, and Shen 2022) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[dCMS2022](#ref-dCMS2022)]; together with the general $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_p$ theorem, this gives the prime-rank cases cited above. In arbitrary rank, Maulik and Shen constructed endoscopic correspondences compatible with the perverse filtrations (Maulik and Shen 2021, Theorem 5.4) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)], without identifying the full filtrations in composite rank. Corollary <a href="#cor:endoscopic-weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:endoscopic-weights">31</a> combines our all-rank equality with cover-side $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_r$ $P=W$ to give the exact Betti-weight image equality asked in (Maulik and Shen 2021, Question 5.5) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)], for the canonical-twist operator transported by nonabelian Hodge theory.

Our argument constructs an operator on the full cohomology, before taking $\Gamma$-invariants. Its algebraic pattern follows the common $\mathfrak{sl}_2$ structure and flag-correspondence methods of Hausel, Mellit, Minets, and Schiffmann (Hausel et al. 2025, Proposition 7.9 and Section 8) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[HMMS2025](#ref-HMMS2025)]. The fixed-determinant construction requires two further geometric steps. First, we pull Mellit’s toric stratifications through a determinant-root cover and prove integral local constancy and ordinary base change. The inputs include the relative product stratification over ordered eigenvalues, including collisions (Mellit 2025, sec. 7.4, Theorem 7.5, and proof of Proposition 8.7) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Mellit2025](#ref-Mellit2025)]. This yields curious hard Lefschetz on the full fixed-determinant cohomology, rather than only its invariant part.

Second, we use a finite diagram of algebraic fibers to give weight zero to the cochains of a topological moving-point base. Shende used this weight convention for simplicial bases and tautological classes (Shende 2017, proof of the main Theorem) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Shende2017](#ref-Shende2017)]. Here the construction also carries variations and proper Gysin maps, using mixed Hodge modules and their coherent enhancement (Saito 1990; Tubach 2025) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[SaitoExtension1990](#ref-SaitoExtension1990), [TubachStacks2025](#ref-TubachStacks2025)]. Its finite weight-graded monodromy supplies the lowering operator. These diagram weights are distinct from the usual weights of an algebraic moving-point curve; that distinction is needed for integration over the curve to have weight shift zero.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The operator behind the comparison

The universal projective bundle on $C\times X$ has local vector-bundle lifts $\mathcal E$. The following characteristic class is independent of the lift and descends to $C\times X$; integration over $C$ gives $$e=\int_C\left(\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2(\mathcal E)-\frac{c_1(\mathcal E)^2}{2n}\right)
=\frac1{2n}\int_C\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2(\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\mathcal E)\in H^2(X).$$ We also write $e$ for cup product by this class. Section <a href="#sec:lefschetz" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lefschetz">2</a> proves that $H^2(X)=\mathbb Qe$ and that $e$ satisfies relative hard Lefschetz, with center $R$. Section <a href="#sec:betti" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:betti">3</a> proves curious hard Lefschetz for the same class and the half-weight grading, again with center $R$.

<a id="intro:operator-goal"></a>
It remains to construct one degree-$-2$ operator $f$ such that $$\label{intro:operator-goal}
fP_k\subset P_{k-2},\qquad
fW_j\subset W_{j-4},\qquad [[f,e],e]=-2e.$$ An elementary Lefschetz-module argument then identifies both filtrations as sums of eigenspaces of the same operator $[e,f]$. It also handles extensions between filtration grades.

To construct $f$, allow one logarithmic pole, move it over a finite cover $S\to C$, and choose a full flag at the pole. The logarithmic $\lambda$-connection parameter interpolates between Higgs bundles at $\lambda=0$ and connections at $\lambda=1$. The underlying moduli constructions and semistable reduction come from (M. A. A. de Cataldo and Fernandez Herrero 2024; Fernandez Herrero and Zhang 2025) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[dCFHLogHodge](#ref-dCFHLogHodge), [FHZ2025](#ref-FHZ2025)]; the restriction argument uses the semiprojective geometry of (Hausel and Rodriguez-Villegas 2015) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[HRV2015](#ref-HRV2015)]. We verify the fixed determinant, residue, and flag conditions, as well as compatibility with the full nonabelian-Hodge identification. Write $X_\lambda$ for the fixed-pole scalar-residue moduli space, with $X_0=X$. At scalar residue, the flag space fits into a correspondence $$X_\lambda\xleftarrow{\ \mathop{\mathrm{pr}}\ }S\times X_\lambda
\xleftarrow{\ \pi\ }Z_\lambda
\xrightarrow{\ \iota\ }Y_{\lambda,0}.$$ Here $Y_{\lambda,0}$ permits a flag-preserving residue with prescribed scalar diagonal, while $Z_\lambda$ requires the residue itself to be scalar. We first deform the ordered residues to distinct values and shift two residue eigenline lattices in opposite directions. Restriction isomorphisms from the total logarithmic Hodge family transport the resulting translation to a degree-zero operator $T$ on $H^*(Y_{\lambda,0})$. On the connection side, $T$ is eigenvalue monodromy. A power has unipotent action, and its logarithm $N$ lowers the diagram weights by two.

At $\lambda=0$, the resulting operator, before multiplication by a nonzero scalar, is $$f'=\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}_*\pi_*\iota^*N^2\iota_*\pi^*\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}^*.$$ The flag shifts cancel. The square $N^2$ lowers weights by four, and integration over $S$ has weight shift zero in the diagram construction. On the Higgs side, nearby cycles realize this same operator as a morphism $Rh_*\mathbb Q_X\to Rh_*\mathbb Q_X[-2]$, giving the perverse bound. A lattice Riemann–Roch calculation then gives the double commutator in <a href="#intro:operator-goal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:operator-goal">[intro:operator-goal]</a> after explicit rescaling.

The proof keeps three comparison statements separate: ordinary base change for the Betti family, restriction isomorphisms for the logarithmic Hodge family, and specialization over the Hitchin base. Each is proved with its own hypotheses. Section <a href="#sec:lefschetz" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:lefschetz">2</a> gives the Lefschetz comparison criterion. Section <a href="#sec:betti" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:betti">3</a> proves ordinary base change and curious hard Lefschetz for the full fixed-determinant Betti family. Section <a href="#sec:diagram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagram">4</a> constructs the diagram weights, and Section <a href="#sec:logarithmic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:logarithmic">5</a> establishes the logarithmic restriction isomorphisms and full-cohomology comparison. Section <a href="#sec:translation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:translation">6</a> constructs the common operator and proves its weight bound; Section <a href="#sec:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:specialization">7</a> proves its perverse bound by specialization. Section <a href="#sec:commutator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:commutator">8</a> computes the double commutator, and Section <a href="#sec:conclusion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:conclusion">9</a> assembles these results to prove Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="sec:lefschetz"></a>
### Lefschetz operators and the comparison criterion

For the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, assume that $n\ge4$ is composite. We first fix the perverse normalization and identify a common candidate for the two Lefschetz operators. We then give the linear-algebra argument that will compare the filtrations once a common lowering operator has been constructed. Throughout this section, cohomology has rational coefficients.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The normalized perverse filtration

Recall that $X$ parametrizes stable, trace-free Higgs bundles of rank $n$ and determinant $L$ on $C$, and that $h:X\to A$ is its Hitchin map. The standard geometry of this system gives a proper surjective morphism from a smooth quasi-projective variety, together with a holomorphic symplectic form of weight one for Higgs-field scaling; see (M. A. A. de Cataldo, Maulik, and Shen 2022, sec. 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[dCMS2022](#ref-dCMS2022)].

<a id="stmt-norm-dimensions"></a>
<div id="norm:dimensions" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *Set $R=(n^2-1)(g-1)$. Then $\dim X=2R$, $\dim A=R$, and every fiber of $h$ has dimension $R$. In particular, $$r:=\dim(X\times_A X)-\dim X=R.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-norm-dimensions"><summary>Proof of stmt-norm-dimensions</summary>

*Proof.* Riemann–Roch gives $$\dim A=\sum_{i=2}^n h^0(C,K_C^i)
 =\sum_{i=2}^n(2i-1)(g-1)=R.$$ The trace-free Higgs deformation complex has vanishing zeroth and second hypercohomology, by stability and Serre duality. Its Euler characteristic is $-2R$, so the tangent space has dimension $2R$ at every point.

We recall why the fiber bound holds also over the singular Hitchin values. Higgs-field scaling makes $X$ semiprojective: properness of $h$ extends each scaling orbit at zero, since its Hitchin value extends there, and the fixed locus is closed in the proper fiber $h^{-1}(0)$. For the Białynicki–Birula decomposition (Białynicki-Birula 1973) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BialynickiBirula1973](#ref-BialynickiBirula1973)] in this semiprojective setting, see (Hausel and Rodriguez-Villegas 2015, sec. 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[HRV2015](#ref-HRV2015)]. At a fixed point, the symplectic form pairs a tangent weight $w$ with weight $1-w$. The weights are integers, so the nonpositive tangent weights account for exactly half the tangent dimension. Consequently each repelling stratum, including its fixed component, has dimension $R$.

The union of these strata is $h^{-1}(0)$. Indeed, a point with a limit at infinity must have zero Hitchin value, because every Hitchin coordinate has positive scaling weight. Conversely, an orbit in $h^{-1}(0)$ has a limit at infinity because that fiber is proper. Thus $\dim h^{-1}(0)=R$. Pulling $h$ back along the weighted scaling curve from any $a\in A$ to zero, upper semicontinuity of fiber dimension for a proper morphism gives $\dim h^{-1}(a)\le R$. The dimension inequality for a morphism from a smooth $2R$-dimensional variety to an $R$-dimensional variety gives the reverse inequality on each nonempty fiber. Surjectivity therefore proves the asserted equidimensionality. It follows that $\dim(X\times_A X)=R+2R=3R$. ◻

</details>

<a id="norm:perverse-range"></a>
Put $$K=Rh_*\mathbb Q_X[R].$$ We normalize the perverse filtration by <a id="norm:perverse-filtration"></a>$$\label{norm:perverse-filtration}
 P_kH^m(X)=\operatorname{Im}\left\{
 H^{m-R}\bigl(A,{}^p\tau_{\le k}K\bigr)
 \longrightarrow H^m(X)\right\}.$$ This is the normalization of (Maulik and Shen 2024, sec. 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[MS2024](#ref-MS2024)]. The fiber bound implies that $Rh_*\mathbb Q_X[2R]$ has perverse cohomology only in degrees $[-R,R]$: the upper bound follows from the support criterion, and the lower bound follows by Verdier duality. Indeed its ordinary stalk cohomology vanishes in positive degrees, and every support has dimension at most $R$. After shifting by $[-R]$, the perverse degrees of $K$ lie in $[0,2R]$. In particular, $$\label{norm:perverse-range}
 P_{-1}H^*(X)=0,\qquad P_{2R}H^*(X)=H^*(X).$$

<a id="norm:shift-rule"></a>
We will use the following shift convention repeatedly. A morphism $K\to K[s]$ induces a map of cohomological degree $s$ sending $P_k$ into $P_{k+s}$, since $$\label{norm:shift-rule}
 {}^p\tau_{\le k}(K[s])
 =({}^p\tau_{\le k+s}K)[s].$$ Thus an actual morphism $Rh_*\mathbb Q_X\to Rh_*\mathbb Q_X[-2]$ will supply precisely the lowering bound needed below.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The degree-two class

The universal projective bundle on $C\times X$ determines the characteristic class $$\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}}_2(E)
 =\operatorname{ch}_2(E)-\frac{c_1(E)^2}{2n}
 =\frac{1}{2n}\operatorname{ch}_2(\operatorname{End}E).$$ The last expression also defines it when a universal vector bundle has not been chosen. Define <a id="norm:e"></a>$$\label{norm:e}
 e=\int_C\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}}_2(E)\in H^2(X).$$ We also write $e$ for cup product by this class.

<a id="stmt-norm-low-cohomology"></a>
<div id="norm:low-cohomology" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3**. *For the composite ranks $n\ge4$ under consideration, $$H^0(X)=\mathbb Q,\qquad H^1(X)=0,\qquad H^2(X)=\mathbb Q e,
