\quad\text{on }H^*(\mathcal Y,\mathbb Q).$$ To compare this operator with the endpoint $F_1$, we need constancy along the two families in <a href="#trans:path" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:path">[trans:path]</a>. Their fibers are nonproper; the restriction isomorphisms, rather than properness, supply the following criterion.

<a id="stmt-log-compact-cycles"></a>
<div id="log:compact-cycles" class="lemma">

**Lemma 21**. *Let $I$ be a connected real interval and let $p:M\to I$ and $q:N\to I$ be smooth submersions, with finite-dimensional rational cohomology on their fibers. Suppose there are spaces $A,B$ and continuous maps $a:M\to A$, $b:N\to B$ whose restrictions induce isomorphisms $$\alpha_t:H^*(A,\mathbb Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^*(M_t,\mathbb Q),
 \qquad
 \beta_t:H^*(B,\mathbb Q)\xrightarrow{\sim}H^*(N_t,\mathbb Q)$$ for every $t\in I$. If $F:M\to N$ is continuous over $I$, then $\alpha_t^{-1}F_t^*\beta_t:H^*(B,\mathbb Q)\to H^*(A,\mathbb Q)$ is independent of $t$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-log-compact-cycles"><summary>Proof of stmt-log-compact-cycles</summary>

*Proof.* Fix a degree and a parameter $t_0$. Choose finitely many compact singular cycles forming a rational homology basis of $M_{t_0}$. A lift of the interval vector field near the compact union of their carriers has a flow for a common short time. It transports these cycles to nearby fibers; the resulting cycles sweep homologies in $M$. Their images in $A$ are therefore homologous, so their pairings with the restrictions of a fixed basis of $H^*(A)$ are constant. The restriction isomorphisms show that the transported cycles remain a basis of the fiber homology.

Applying the continuous map $bF$ to these swept cycles gives homologies in $B$. Hence their pairings with every fixed class of $H^*(B)$ are also constant. These pairings are the matrix coefficients of $\alpha_t^{-1}F_t^*\beta_t$, relative to the fixed bases. The operator is thus locally constant, including one-sided neighborhoods of interval endpoints, and therefore constant on $I$. ◻

</details>

<a id="trans:endpoint"></a>
Apply the lemma with $I=[0,1]$ and with $M,N$ the pullbacks of $\mathcal Y\to\Lambda$ along $$a\longmapsto(a,v),\qquad a\longmapsto(a,v-a\ell),$$ respectively. These are smooth submersions by Proposition <a href="#log:families" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:families">14</a>, and <a href="#trans:path" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:path">[trans:path]</a> gives the continuous map $F:M\to N$. Take both ambient spaces in the lemma to be $\mathcal Y$, with the natural maps from the two pullbacks. Their fiber restrictions induce the isomorphisms of Corollary <a href="#log:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:restriction">15</a>. The constancy conclusion therefore gives $$\label{trans:endpoint}
\mathsf T=\rho_{1,v}^{-1}F_1^*\rho_{1,v-\ell}.$$ In particular, this identification does not require the nonproper smooth families to be globally locally trivial.

Recall the eigenvalue map $$t(u)=(\zeta e^{-2\pi\sqrt{-1}u_i})_{i=1}^n.$$ The path $u=v-a\ell$, $0\le a\le1$, maps to a loop in $V$. Every forbidden proper subproduct has modulus different from one because the corresponding partial sum of $v$ has nonzero imaginary part. The same condition excludes resonance along the entire path. By Proposition <a href="#log:rh" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:rh">16</a>, this is a path in the actual moving-puncture Betti family. Restrictions of classes from $\mathcal Y$ are flat sections along this path: the Betti direct images satisfy ordinary base change and are locally constant. Since $F_1$ is the endpoint identity, <a href="#trans:endpoint" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:endpoint">[trans:endpoint]</a> identifies $\mathsf T$ with the pullback convention for its eigenvalue monodromy.

<a id="stmt-trans-logarithm"></a>
<div id="trans:logarithm" class="lemma">

<a id="trans:N"></a>
**Lemma 22**. *There is an integer $M>0$ such that $$\label{trans:N}
N=\frac1M\log(\mathsf T^M)$$ is a well-defined nilpotent derivation of $H^*(\mathcal Y,\mathbb Q)$. Let $N_{\lambda,u}=\rho_{\lambda,u}N\rho_{\lambda,u}^{-1}$. On $H^*(Y_{1,0},\mathbb Q)$, equipped with the diagram weights of Section <a href="#sec:diagram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagram">4</a>, one has $$N_{1,0}W_j\subset W_{j-2}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-trans-logarithm"><summary>Proof of stmt-trans-logarithm</summary>

*Proof.* The full-flag Betti family has integral lisse direct images, ordinary base change, and Hodge–Tate fibers by Section <a href="#sec:betti" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:betti">3</a>. The moving-puncture transition maps are algebraic and locally constant by Proposition <a href="#log:rh" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:rh">16</a>. The finite-monodromy statement of Section <a href="#sec:diagram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagram">4</a> therefore applies to this very family. Choose $M$ that kills the monodromy action on every weight-graded piece of its total cohomology. There are finitely many such pieces. Then $\mathsf T^M-1$ is nilpotent, and <a href="#trans:N" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:N">[trans:N]</a> is a finite logarithmic sum. The weights are even, so $N$ lowers them by at least two at $(1,v)$.

The path $(1,av)$, $0\le a\le1$, stays in the nonresonant inverse image of $V$. At $a=0$, the scalar point belongs to $V$ because $\zeta$ has order $n$. For $a>0$, the imaginary-part argument applies again. Parallel transport preserves the diagram weights and coincides with the restriction identifications. This gives the stated estimate at $(1,0)$.

Lemma <a href="#diag:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="diag:finite">12</a> makes the logarithm a derivation on the moving-puncture Betti cohomology. The restriction identifications are ring isomorphisms, so this property transports to $H^*(\mathcal Y,\mathbb Q)$. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-25"></a>
#### Flag push-pull and the weight bound

<a id="trans:fprime"></a>
For the scalar correspondence at $\lambda$, put $$\mathsf A_\lambda=\pi_{\lambda,*}\iota_\lambda^*,
\qquad
\mathsf B_\lambda=\iota_{\lambda,*}\pi_\lambda^*.$$ Its unflagged moving-pole space is $S\times X_\lambda$, with projection $\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}_\lambda$. We define $$\label{trans:fprime}
f'=\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}_{0,*}\,\mathsf A_0 N_{0,0}^2\mathsf B_0\,\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}_0^*
:H^m(X,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow H^{m-2}(X,\mathbb Q).$$ The flag embedding and projection have opposite degree shifts, $2b$ and $-2b$. Integration over the compact curve $S$ supplies the remaining degree $-2$. Table <a href="#spec:shift-table" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:shift-table">1</a> records the individual derived morphisms and their degree and weight shifts. Section <a href="#sec:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:specialization">7</a> derives the perverse bound from their composite over the Hitchin base. We first prove the weight bound.

<a id="stmt-trans-weights"></a>
<div id="trans:weights" class="proposition">

**Proposition 23**. *Under nonabelian Hodge theory, the operator <a href="#trans:fprime" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:fprime">[trans:fprime]</a> satisfies $$f'W_jH^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)\subset W_{j-4}H^{m-2}(X^B,\mathbb Q).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-trans-weights"><summary>Proof of stmt-trans-weights</summary>

*Proof.* The scalar correspondence and its Gysin maps commute with the restriction identifications of Corollary <a href="#log:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:restriction">15</a>. Thus <a href="#trans:fprime" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:fprime">[trans:fprime]</a> transports to the same expression at $\lambda=1$. By Proposition <a href="#log:nah" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:nah">19</a>, these identifications transport the ordinary Betti weight filtration on the full cohomology of $X^B$. The possible difference from a chosen nonabelian-Hodge identification is one algebraic $\Gamma$-action, which preserves every weight subspace, including those in variant cohomology.

At $\lambda=1$, the scalar Betti space is the flat algebraic product $S\times X^B$ of Lemma <a href="#log:scalar-flat-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:scalar-flat-product">17</a>. Its diagram mixed Hodge structure assigns weight zero to the cochains of $S$ and the ordinary Deligne weights to $H^*(X^B)$. Hence both $\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}_1^*$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{pr}}_{1,*}$ preserve weights. The flag maps are maps of the same flat transition diagrams. Their ordinary topological Gysin maps are the diagram Gysin maps, with weight shifts $2b$ for $\mathsf B_1$ and $-2b$ for $\mathsf A_1$. These shifts cancel, while $N_{1,0}^2$ lowers weight by four by Lemma <a href="#trans:logarithm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="trans:logarithm">22</a>. ◻

</details>

We have constructed the operator and proved its weight bound. The remaining tasks are to realize it over the Hitchin base, which gives the perverse bound, and to compute its double commutator with $e$.

<a id="sec:specialization"></a>
### Specialization over a Higgs line

We now prove the perverse-filtration bound for the operator $f'$ in <a href="#trans:fprime" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:fprime">[trans:fprime]</a>. The translation is defined over a logarithmic Hitchin base away from zero on a Higgs line. Nearby cycles extend its action to the zero fiber as a sheaf endomorphism. A final integration over the moving-point curve supplies the shift by $-2$. The essential comparison with ordinary cohomology uses the restriction isomorphisms of Corollary <a href="#log:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:restriction">15</a>; it does not require the parameter morphism to be proper.

<a id="pm-h-27"></a>
#### The proper morphism and its relative translation

Keep the vector $v$ and the translation from Section <a href="#sec:translation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:translation">6</a>. Let $p_C:C\times S\to S$ be the projection and let $\Delta\subset C\times S$ be the graph of $x:S\to C$. The vector bundle of trace-free logarithmic Hitchin coefficients is $$\mathcal E=
 \bigoplus_{i=2}^n
 p_{C,*}\bigl(K_{C\times S/S}(\Delta)^{\otimes i}\bigr).$$ We use the same notation for its total space. These direct images are locally free and commute with base change: each twisting line on a fiber has degree $i(2g-1)>2g-2$, so its first cohomology vanishes. The inclusion of ordinary coefficient spaces defines a closed vector-subbundle embedding $$j:S\times A\hookrightarrow\mathcal E.$$ Indeed both the ordinary spaces and the logarithmic spaces commute with base change, and their quotient has constant rank.

<a id="spec:hitchin-line"></a>
Let $D=\mathbb A^1_z$, let $D^*=D\setminus\{0\}$, and define $$\mathcal Y_v=\mathcal Y\times_{\Lambda}D,
 \qquad z\longmapsto(0,zv).$$ Thus its fiber at $z$ is $Y_{0,zv}$, including the moving-point coordinate in $S$. Characteristic coefficients and the parameter define $$\label{spec:hitchin-line}
 g_v:\mathcal Y_v\longrightarrow\mathcal E\times D.$$ Write $g_0:Y_{0,0}\to\mathcal E$ for its zero fiber.

<a id="stmt-spec-geometry"></a>
<div id="spec:geometry" class="lemma">

**Lemma 24**. *The morphism $g_v$ is proper, and $\mathcal Y_v\to D$ is smooth. The translation defines an automorphism $T_v$ of $\mathcal Y_v|_{D^*}$ over $\mathcal E\times D^*$. Scaling gives an isomorphism $$\mathcal Y_v|_{D^*}\simeq Y_{0,v}\times D^*$$ over $D^*$, and restriction from $H^*(\mathcal Y_v)$ to either $Y_{0,0}$ or $Y_{0,v}$ is an isomorphism.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-geometry"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-geometry</summary>

*Proof.* Smoothness and the restriction assertions follow from Proposition <a href="#log:families" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:families">14</a> and Corollary <a href="#log:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:restriction">15</a>. For properness, start with the relative logarithmic Higgs Hitchin morphism over $S$. Imposing determinant $\mathcal O_C(dx(s))$ and trace zero gives a closed subspace. Adding a residue-invariant full flag is projective over that subspace. Finally the requirement that the ordered diagonal residues equal $zv_i$ is closed after adjoining $z$. The properness of the logarithmic Hitchin morphism from Section <a href="#sec:logarithmic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:logarithmic">5</a> therefore implies properness of <a href="#spec:hitchin-line" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:hitchin-line">[spec:hitchin-line]</a>. Coprimality ensures that the semistable limits in this argument remain stable.

For $z\ne0$ the residual eigenvalues $zv_i$ are distinct, so the elementary modifications defining $T_v$ and their inverses are algebraic in $z$. They remain in the stable moduli by the trace-residue argument of Section <a href="#sec:translation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:translation">6</a>. A modified Higgs field agrees with the original meromorphic Higgs field away from the pole. Their characteristic coefficients are consequently equal as sections of $K_C(x(s))^{\otimes i}$, which proves that $T_v$ is over $\mathcal E$. Multiplying the Higgs field by $z$ identifies the fiber at $1$ with that at $z$; division by $z$ is its inverse. This gives the claimed product over $D^*$. The product need not fix the Hitchin coefficient in $\mathcal E$; only the translation must do so. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-28"></a>
#### Specialization and ordinary cohomology

Here is the comparison needed for the noncompact spaces in Lemma <a href="#spec:geometry" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:geometry">24</a>. Throughout, nearby cycles are unshifted; we use the nearby-cycle and proper-base-change formalism recalled in (M. A. A. de Cataldo and Migliorini 2009, sec. 5.5 and 5.8) <a id="pm-cite-55"></a>[[dCM2009](#ref-dCM2009)].

<a id="stmt-spec-specialization"></a>
<div id="spec:specialization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 25**. *Let $Y\to D=\mathbb A^1$ be a smooth morphism of complex algebraic varieties, and let $g:Y\to B\times D$ be a proper morphism commuting with the maps to $D$. Suppose that $Y|_{D^*}$ is isomorphic to $Y_1\times D^*$ over $D^*$ and that the actual restriction maps $$r_i:H^*(Y,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow H^*(Y_i,\mathbb Q),
 \qquad i=0,1,$$ are isomorphisms. An automorphism $T$ of $Y|_{D^*}$ over $B\times D^*$ then induces an endomorphism $\tau_0$ of $Rg_{0,*}\mathbb Q_{Y_0}$ whose cohomological action is $$H^*(\tau_0)=r_0r_1^{-1}T_1^*r_1r_0^{-1}.$$ Neither $B$ nor $Y_i$ is required to be proper.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-specialization"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-specialization</summary>

*Proof.* Smoothness gives the canonical isomorphism $\mathbb Q_{Y_0}\simeq\psi\mathbb Q_Y$. Compatibility of nearby cycles with proper direct image gives $$Rg_{0,*}\mathbb Q_{Y_0}
 \simeq Rg_{0,*}\psi\mathbb Q_Y
 \simeq\psi(Rg_*\mathbb Q_Y).$$ Apply nearby cycles to the pullback endomorphism induced by $T$ on the punctured direct image, and use these isomorphisms to define $\tau_0$.

<a id="spec:comparison-map"></a>
We verify its action on global cohomology without interchanging nearby cycles with a nonproper direct image to a point. Put $K=Rg_*\mathbb Q_Y$ on $B\times D$. Let $\widetilde D^*\to D^*$ be the universal cover and write $$\widetilde j_B:B\times\widetilde D^*\longrightarrow B\times D,
 \qquad i_B:B\times\{0\}\hookrightarrow B\times D,
 \qquad \widetilde K=\widetilde j_B^*K.$$ Set $\widetilde Y^*=Y|_{D^*}\times_{D^*}\widetilde D^*$. Proper base change identifies $\widetilde K$ with the direct image of the constant sheaf from $\widetilde Y^*$. The nearby-cycle formula downstairs is $\psi K=i_B^*R\widetilde j_{B,*}\widetilde K$. Restriction of this complex gives a natural morphism $$\label{spec:comparison-map}
 \begin{split}
 R\Gamma(\widetilde Y^*,\mathbb Q)
 &\simeq R\Gamma(B\times D,R\widetilde j_{B,*}\widetilde K)\\
 &\longrightarrow R\Gamma(B,\psi K)
 \simeq R\Gamma(Y_0,\mathbb Q).
 \end{split}$$ The last isomorphism uses proper nearby-cycle compatibility and smoothness of $Y\to D$. The composite of <a href="#spec:comparison-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:comparison-map">[spec:comparison-map]</a> with restriction from $R\Gamma(Y,\mathbb Q)$ is ordinary restriction to $Y_0$. Indeed the specialization unit for $K$ corresponds, under proper direct image, to the unit $\mathbb Q_{Y_0}\to\psi\mathbb Q_Y$, which is the canonical smooth isomorphism used above.

Choose a lift of $1\in D^*$. The assumed product and the contractibility of $\widetilde D^*$ identify $H^*(\widetilde Y^*)$ with $H^*(Y_1)$ by actual restriction to that lift. Pullback from $H^*(Y)$ therefore becomes $r_1$ and is an isomorphism. Since its composite with <a href="#spec:comparison-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:comparison-map">[spec:comparison-map]</a> is $r_0$, the latter map is also an isomorphism on cohomology.

The comparison was constructed on $B\times D$ so that it is equivariant for the required operator: because $T$ is over $B\times D^*$, it induces an endomorphism of $K|_{B\times D^*}$ and hence of $\widetilde K$, $R\widetilde j_{B,*}\widetilde K$, and $\psi K$. Every arrow in <a href="#spec:comparison-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:comparison-map">[spec:comparison-map]</a> respects these actions. Restricting at the chosen lift identifies the action on its domain with $T_1^*$, and its action on the codomain is $H^*(\tau_0)$. Thus the displayed formula for $H^*(\tau_0)$ follows. No extension of $T$ to an automorphism of $Y$ is asserted or needed. ◻

</details>

Apply Lemma <a href="#spec:specialization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:specialization">25</a> to <a href="#spec:hitchin-line" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:hitchin-line">[spec:hitchin-line]</a>. The resulting endomorphism $\tau_0$ of $Rg_{0,*}\mathbb Q_{Y_{0,0}}$ acts on cohomology as the translation transported to $Y_{0,0}$ by Corollary <a href="#log:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:restriction">15</a>. In particular its action is the operator whose logarithm is $N_{0,0}$ in Section <a href="#sec:translation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:translation">6</a>.

A finite polynomial suffices to realize this logarithm over the Hitchin base. Choose $M$ as in Lemma <a href="#trans:logarithm" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="trans:logarithm">22</a>, and choose an integer $r\ge1$ for which $(\mathsf T^M-1)^r=0$ on total cohomology. Put $$p(t)=\frac1M\sum_{a=1}^{r-1}
       \frac{(-1)^{a+1}}{a}(t^M-1)^a.$$ The polynomial $p(\tau_0)$ is a legitimate endomorphism of $Rg_{0,*}\mathbb Q_{Y_{0,0}}$, and its action on cohomology is $N_{0,0}$. No unipotence assertion about $\tau_0$ in the derived category is needed.

<a id="pm-h-29"></a>
#### The derived shift

We have now realized the middle operator in $f'$ over the Hitchin base. We next include the flag correspondence and the moving-point integration, keeping every shift explicit.

<a id="stmt-spec-perverse-lowering"></a>
<div id="spec:perverse-lowering" class="proposition">

<a id="spec:derived-lowering"></a>
**Proposition 26**. *The operator $f'$ of <a href="#trans:fprime" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:fprime">[trans:fprime]</a> is induced by a morphism $$\label{spec:derived-lowering}
 Rh_*\mathbb Q_X\longrightarrow Rh_*\mathbb Q_X[-2]$$ in the constructible derived category on $A$. Consequently $$f'(P_kH^m(X,\mathbb Q))\subseteq P_{k-2}H^{m-2}(X,\mathbb Q)$$ for all integers $m,k$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-spec-perverse-lowering"><summary>Proof of stmt-spec-perverse-lowering</summary>

*Proof.* Let $O_0=S\times X$ using the scalar product identification of Lemma <a href="#log:scalar-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:scalar-product">13</a>, and write $q=\operatorname{id}_S\times h$. Both maps in the scalar flag correspondence of Figure <a href="#log:scalar-correspondence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:scalar-correspondence">1</a> at $\lambda=0$ are over $\mathcal E$. The flag projection has relative complex dimension $b$, and the scalar inclusion has complex codimension $b$. Thus their pullbacks and proper Gysin maps give, with $$\begin{gathered}
 F_O=j_*Rq_*\mathbb Q_{O_0},\qquad
 F_Y=Rg_{0,*}\mathbb Q_{Y_{0,0}},\\
 F_Z=R(g_0\circ\iota)_*\mathbb Q_{Z_0},
 \end{gathered}$$ morphisms $$\mathsf B_0:F_O\longrightarrow F_Y[2b],
 \qquad
 \mathsf A_0:F_Y\longrightarrow F_O[-2b].$$ Composing these with the appropriate shifts of $p(\tau_0)^2$ gives an endomorphism of $F_O$ with zero net shift. Its cohomological action is $\mathsf A_0N_{0,0}^2\mathsf B_0$. Since direct image by the closed embedding $j$ is fully faithful, this is an endomorphism of $Rq_*\mathbb Q_{O_0}$ over $S\times A$.

Let $p_S:S\times A\to A$ be the projection, and write $K=Rh_*\mathbb Q_X$. The product identification gives $$Rp_{S,*}Rq_*\mathbb Q_{O_0}
 \simeq K\otimes R\Gamma(S,\mathbb Q).$$ Push the preceding endomorphism to $A$, precompose with the projection unit $K\to K\otimes R\Gamma(S,\mathbb Q)$, and postcompose with the orientation trace $$K\otimes R\Gamma(S,\mathbb Q)\longrightarrow K[-2].$$ This constructs <a href="#spec:derived-lowering" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:derived-lowering">[spec:derived-lowering]</a>. On ordinary cohomology the unit is $\operatorname{pr}_0^*$, the trace is $\operatorname{pr}_{0,*}$, and the middle map is the flag composite already identified. Hence the resulting operator is exactly $f'$.

For completeness, set $K_{\mathrm{norm}}=K[R]$, the normalization used in the statement of the theorem. Functoriality of perverse truncation applied to <a href="#spec:derived-lowering" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="spec:derived-lowering">[spec:derived-lowering]</a> gives $${}^p\tau_{\le k}K_{\mathrm{norm}}
 \longrightarrow{}^p\tau_{\le k}(K_{\mathrm{norm}}[-2])
 =({}^p\tau_{\le k-2}K_{\mathrm{norm}})[-2].$$ Taking hypercohomology in degree $m-R$ and then its image in ordinary cohomology proves the stated inclusion. This uses neither a splitting nor multiplicativity of the perverse filtration. ◻

</details>

We finish by recording the derived morphisms and their shifts. Retain the complexes $F_O,F_Z,F_Y$ and $K$ from the proof, and put $K_S=K\otimes R\Gamma(S,\mathbb Q)$.

The first panel of Table <a href="#spec:shift-table" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:shift-table">1</a> consists of morphisms on $\mathcal E$. Compose the flag maps and $p(\tau_0)^2$ there to obtain an endomorphism of $F_O$, descend it through $j$, and then apply $Rp_{S,*}$. This produces the degree-zero endomorphism of $K_S$ in the second panel, whose base is $A$. The unit and trace then give $K\to K[-2]$; it is this complete morphism that yields the normalized perverse bound above. No perverse exactness of $Rp_{S,*}$ is used. The weight columns refer to the cohomological actions after transport to $\lambda=1$ and use the diagram weights of Section <a href="#sec:diagram" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:diagram">4</a>. In particular $p(\tau_0)$ acts as $N_{1,0}$ after this comparison, while the unit and trace preserve weight because the cochains of $S$ have weight zero.

<div id="spec:shift-table">

| Morphisms on $\mathcal E$ |                   |        |            |
|:--------------------------|:------------------|-------:|-----------:|
| Operation                 | Morphism          | Degree | $\Delta W$ |
| $\pi_0^*$                 | $F_O\to F_Z$      |    $0$ |        $0$ |
| $\iota_{0,*}$             | $F_Z\to F_Y[2b]$  |   $2b$ |       $2b$ |
| $p(\tau_0)$               | $F_Y\to F_Y$      |    $0$ |       $-2$ |
| $p(\tau_0)^2$             | $F_Y\to F_Y$      |    $0$ |       $-4$ |
| $\iota_0^*$               | $F_Y\to F_Z$      |    $0$ |        $0$ |
| $\pi_{0,*}$               | $F_Z\to F_O[-2b]$ |  $-2b$ |      $-2b$ |
| Flag composite            | $F_O\to F_O$      |    $0$ |       $-4$ |
| Morphisms on $A$          |                   |        |            |
| Operation                 | Morphism          | Degree | $\Delta W$ |
| Projection unit           | $K\to K_S$        |    $0$ |        $0$ |
| Pushed composite          | $K_S\to K_S$      |    $0$ |       $-4$ |
| Orientation trace         | $K_S\to K[-2]$    |   $-2$ |        $0$ |
| Complete $f'$             | $K\to K[-2]$      |   $-2$ |       $-4$ |

Derived degrees and diagram-weight bounds for the correspondence maps. A weight bound $s$ means that $W_k$ maps into $W_{k+s}$ after transport to the Betti side. The row for $p(\tau_0)$ records $N$ separately; the flag composite uses its square.

</div>

<a id="sec:commutator"></a>
### The translation class and the double commutator

The preceding sections give the two filtration bounds for the same operator $f'$. We now compute its double commutator with $e$. The calculation has two parts: an elementary modification differentiates the universal degree-two class into a difference of flag classes, and flag integration converts the square of that difference back into $e$.

<a id="pm-h-31"></a>
#### The characteristic class under translation

The universal projective bundle on $C\times\mathcal Y$ defines a universal endomorphism bundle. Let $$e_{\mathcal Y}
   =\frac1{2n}\int_C\operatorname{ch}_2(\operatorname{End}E)
   \in H^2(\mathcal Y,\mathbb Q).$$ Individual ordered flag lines $Q_i$ can be taken on local universal-bundle charts; their ratios $Q_i\otimes Q_j^{-1}$ descend to the coarse moduli space. We use the global classes $$y=c_1(Q_1\otimes Q_2^{-1}),\qquad
 t_C=x^*c_1(T_C),$$ where the second is pulled back from $S$. We retain the same notation for the restrictions of these classes to a fiber $Y_{\lambda,u}$.

<a id="stmt-calc-translation-formula"></a>
<div id="calc:translation-formula" class="lemma">

<a id="calc:translation-polynomial"></a>
<a id="calc:logarithm"></a>
**Lemma 27**. *For the translation $T:Y_{0,v}\to Y_{0,v}$ associated with $\ell=(1,-1,0,\ldots,0)$ and every integer $k\ge0$, $$\label{calc:translation-polynomial}
 T^{k*}e_{\mathcal Y}=e_{\mathcal Y}+ky+k^2t_C.$$ Consequently the logarithm from Section <a href="#sec:translation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:translation">6</a> satisfies $$\label{calc:logarithm}
 N(e_{\mathcal Y})=y$$ on $H^*(\mathcal Y,\mathbb Q)$ and, by restriction, on every fiber.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-calc-translation-formula"><summary>Proof of stmt-calc-translation-formula</summary>

*Proof.* Work first on $Y_{0,v}$. Denote by $i:\Delta\hookrightarrow C\times Y_{0,v}$ the graph of the moving point. Its normal line is the pullback of $x^*T_C$, so $$\mathcal O(\Delta)|_\Delta\simeq x^*T_C,
 \qquad c_1(N_\Delta)=t_C.$$ The residue eigenvalues $v_i$ are distinct. Consequently the Higgs operator has uniquely labeled eigenline summands on the formal neighborhood of $\Delta$. This follows by lifting its simple residual eigenvalues and their idempotent projectors to every finite order. Multiplying a local frame of the logarithmic canonical line by a unit does not change those projectors. Thus the formal summands are intrinsic, and any finite number of their jets suffices for an elementary modification.

<a id="calc:signed-jets"></a>
The $k$-fold translation replaces the $i$th formal line lattice by its twist by $k\ell_i\Delta$. On the $(i,j)$ block of $\operatorname{End}E$, the lattice shift is therefore $$m_{ij}=k(\ell_i-\ell_j).$$ If a line block has restriction $F$ to $\Delta$, its K-theory change under an integer shift $m$ is $$\label{calc:signed-jets}
 [\text{new block}]-[\text{old block}]
   =\sum_{a=1}^{m}[i_*(F\otimes N_\Delta^{\otimes a})].$$ For $m<0$, the right side means the negative of the sum over $m<a\le0$; for $m=0$ it is zero. For positive $m$, the formula follows by successive quotients of the lattices at pole orders $1,\ldots,m$. For negative $m$, apply the same quotient sequence between the smaller lattice and the original one. In particular $m=-1$ contributes $-[i_*F]$, as it must for the kernel elementary transformation.

The resulting identity for $\operatorname{End}E$ is global in K-theory, before applying a characteristic class. Indeed the intrinsic formal idempotents lie in the descended endomorphism algebra, so its $(i,j)$ blocks are global formal line bundles. Identify the original and modified endomorphism bundles away from $\Delta$, and choose a common sufficiently small lattice inside both. Their quotients by this lattice are supported on a finite infinitesimal neighborhood of $\Delta$. The formal block projectors and the filtration by pole order give global finite filtrations of these quotients. Their associated graded terms are graph pushforwards of the descended line bundles $Q_i\otimes Q_j^{-1}\otimes N_\Delta^{\otimes a}$. Subtracting the two quotient classes cancels the common pole orders and leaves exactly the signed sums in <a href="#calc:signed-jets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:signed-jets">[calc:signed-jets]</a>. Additivity for these filtrations therefore gives the identity in the Grothendieck group of coherent sheaves on $C\times Y_{0,v}$; the common lattice also cancels.

Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch for the graph (Fulton 1998, vol. 2, sec. 15.2) <a id="pm-cite-56"></a>[[Fulton1998](#ref-Fulton1998)] gives $$\operatorname{ch}(i_*F)
   =i_*\bigl(\operatorname{ch}(F)\operatorname{td}(N_\Delta)^{-1}\bigr).$$ Hence the contribution of a summand in <a href="#calc:signed-jets" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:signed-jets">[calc:signed-jets]</a> to $\int_C\operatorname{ch}_2$ is $$c_1(F)+(a-\tfrac12)t_C.$$ This also exhibits the sign of the normal-bundle correction: the normal line is the tangent line, and its inverse Todd class starts with $1-t_C/2$. For the signed sums just specified, the elementary identities $$\sum_{a=1}^{m}1=m,
 \qquad
 \sum_{a=1}^{m}(a-\tfrac12)=\frac{m^2}{2}$$ hold for every integer $m$.

Write $w_i=c_1(Q_i)$ on a universal-bundle chart. Only $w_i-w_j$ and expressions invariant under common translation of the $w_i$ will occur. Summing the contributions for $F=Q_i\otimes Q_j^{-1}$ gives $$T^{k*}e_{\mathcal Y}-e_{\mathcal Y}
 =\frac1{2n}\sum_{i,j}
   \left(k(\ell_i-\ell_j)(w_i-w_j)
       +\frac{k^2}{2}(\ell_i-\ell_j)^2t_C\right).$$ Since $\sum_i\ell_i=0$, $$\sum_{i,j}(\ell_i-\ell_j)(w_i-w_j)=2n\sum_i\ell_iw_i,
 \qquad
 \sum_{i,j}(\ell_i-\ell_j)^2=2n\sum_i\ell_i^2.$$ For the chosen cocharacter these are $2ny$ and $4n$, respectively, proving <a href="#calc:translation-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:translation-polynomial">[calc:translation-polynomial]</a>. Since the lattice identity was global on the coarse moduli space before applying Grothendieck–Riemann–Roch, this is a global cohomological identity there.

Let $M$ be the integer used to define $N$. Replace $k$ by $Mq$ in <a href="#calc:translation-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:translation-polynomial">[calc:translation-polynomial]</a>. Since $\mathsf T^{Mq}=\exp(MqN)$, both sides are polynomial functions of the nonnegative integer $q$ with values in a finite-dimensional cohomology group. They agree as polynomials. Their linear coefficients are respectively $MN(e_{\mathcal
Y})$ and $My$, giving <a href="#calc:logarithm" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:logarithm">[calc:logarithm]</a> on $Y_{0,v}$. Corollary <a href="#log:restriction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:restriction">15</a> transports this identity to $H^*(\mathcal Y)$ and then to all its fibers: the three classes in <a href="#calc:translation-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:translation-polynomial">[calc:translation-polynomial]</a> are restrictions of the global classes just defined. ◻

</details>

The quadratic term in <a href="#calc:translation-polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:translation-polynomial">[calc:translation-polynomial]</a> is consistent with the behavior of the flag lines themselves. Restricting a shifted lattice to $\Delta$ gives $$T^{k*}y=y+2kt_C,
 \qquad N(y)=2t_C,
 \qquad N(t_C)=0.$$ In particular one must not assume $N(y)=0$ in the commutator calculation.

<a id="pm-h-32"></a>
#### The flag integral

We record explicitly the permutation identity that evaluates the flag correspondence. It applies to projective bundles because every polynomial used below is invariant under simultaneous translation of the roots.

<a id="stmt-calc-flag-sum"></a>
<div id="calc:flag-sum" class="lemma">

<a id="calc:permutation"></a>
**Lemma 28**. *Let $\pi:\operatorname{Fl}(V)\to B$ be the full-flag bundle of a rank-$n$ complex vector bundle, and let $w_1,\ldots,w_n$ be its formal Chern roots. For [(calc:permutation)](#calc:permutation) a polynomial $F$ in the first Chern classes of the ordered flag lines, $$\pi_*\bigl(F(Q_\bullet)c_b(T_\pi)\bigr)
   =\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak S_n}
      F(w_{\sigma(1)},\ldots,w_{\sigma(n)}).$$ If $\sum_i\ell_i=0$, then $$\label{calc:permutation}
 \sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak S_n}
       \left(\sum_i\ell_iw_{\sigma(i)}\right)^2
 =n(n-2)!\|\ell\|^2
    \left(\sum_iw_i^2-\frac{(\sum_iw_i)^2}{n}\right).$$ Both identities, when their expressions are invariant under common translation of the roots, hold for the associated flag bundle of a principal $\mathrm{PGL}_n$-bundle with rational coefficients.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-calc-flag-sum"><summary>Proof of stmt-calc-flag-sum</summary>

*Proof.* The projective-bundle pushforward formula is a sum over the roots, with denominator the product of the other-root differences. Iterating it along the full-flag tower gives the sum over ordered roots, with denominator the tangent Euler class at that ordering. Multiplication by $c_b(T_\pi)$ cancels this denominator, giving the first identity. One may perform this calculation after passing to a splitting space and inverting root differences; the resulting equality is a polynomial identity in the universal Chern classes, so it holds without the inversion. The projective-bundle pushforward is characterized by its Chern-polynomial relation and the normalization that the top power of the hyperplane class integrates to one; using the complex tangent Euler class fixes the orientation signs in the displayed cancellation.

For <a href="#calc:permutation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:permutation">[calc:permutation]</a>, a fixed root occurs $(n-1)!$ times at a fixed position, and a fixed ordered pair of distinct roots occurs $(n-2)!$ times at two prescribed distinct positions. Since $\sum_{i\ne j}\ell_i\ell_j=-\|\ell\|^2$, expansion of the square gives $$\begin{split}
 &(n-1)!\|\ell\|^2\sum_iw_i^2
 -(n-2)!\|\ell\|^2\sum_{i\ne j}w_iw_j\\
 &\hspace{15mm}
 =n(n-2)!\|\ell\|^2
    \left(\sum_iw_i^2-\frac{(\sum_iw_i)^2}{n}\right).
 \end{split}$$ Finally, rational pullback from $B\mathrm{PGL}_n$ to $B\mathrm{GL}_n$ is injective: on a maximal torus it is the inclusion of symmetric polynomials in the root differences into symmetric polynomials in all roots. Universal characteristic-class identities invariant under common root translation can therefore be checked after this pullback, proving the last assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-33"></a>
#### The common lowering operator

<a id="stmt-calc-double-commutator"></a>
<div id="calc:double-commutator" class="proposition">

<a id="calc:scalar"></a>
<a id="calc:f"></a>
**Proposition 29**. *Let $\delta=\deg(x:S\to C)$. The operator $f'$ of <a href="#trans:fprime" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="trans:fprime">[trans:fprime]</a> satisfies $$\label{calc:scalar}
 [[f',e],e]=8(-1)^b n(n-2)!\delta\,e.$$ Consequently $$\label{calc:f}
 f=\frac{(-1)^{b+1}}{4n(n-2)!\delta}\,f'$$ has cohomological degree $-2$, lowers perversity by two and Betti weights by four, and satisfies $[[f,e],e]=-2e$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-calc-double-commutator"><summary>Proof of stmt-calc-double-commutator</summary>

*Proof.* Work on the scalar correspondence at $\lambda=0$. The restriction of $e_{\mathcal Y}$ along $\iota:Z_0\hookrightarrow Y_{0,0}$ equals $\pi^*\operatorname{pr}_0^*e$: the universal projective characteristic class is unchanged by the line twist $J$ in the scalar product identification. Projection formulas therefore show that the maps outside $N_{0,0}^2$ in $f'$ intertwine multiplication by the corresponding classes $e$ and $e_{\mathcal Y}$.

<a id="calc:middle-commutator"></a>
For a degree-zero derivation $N$ and an even class $a$, write $m_a$ for multiplication by $a$. Then $[N,m_a]=m_{N(a)}$, and direct expansion gives $$[[N^2,m_a],m_a]=2m_{N(a)^2}.$$ Indeed the first commutator is $m_{N^2(a)}+2m_{N(a)}N$; the first term commutes with $m_a$, while the second contributes $2m_{N(a)^2}$. Thus no vanishing assertion about $N^2(a)$ is needed. By Lemma <a href="#calc:translation-formula" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="calc:translation-formula">27</a>, $$\label{calc:middle-commutator}
 [[f',e],e]
 =2\operatorname{pr}_{0,*}\mathsf A_0
       (y^2\cup-)\mathsf B_0\operatorname{pr}_0^*.$$

The normal bundle of $\iota$ is $T_\pi^*$. Self-intersection therefore replaces $\iota^*\iota_*$ in <a href="#calc:middle-commutator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:middle-commutator">[calc:middle-commutator]</a> by $$c_b(T_\pi^*)\cup-=(-1)^b c_b(T_\pi)\cup-.$$ For a class pulled back from $X$, the projection formula and Lemma <a href="#calc:flag-sum" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="calc:flag-sum">28</a> reduce the flag integral to the symmetric class $$\begin{split}
 \sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak S_n}
     (w_{\sigma(1)}-w_{\sigma(2)})^2
 &=2n(n-2)!
    \left(\sum_iw_i^2-\frac{(\sum_iw_i)^2}{n}\right)\\
 &=4n(n-2)!\,\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}}_2(E_{x(s)}).
 \end{split}$$ Here $E_{x(s)}$ denotes the universal projective data restricted to the graph of $x$; the normalized expression is well defined without an honest universal vector bundle.

Under $O_0\simeq S\times X$, this characteristic class is the pullback of $\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}}_2(E)$ from $C\times X$ along $x\times\operatorname{id}_X$. Tensoring by $J$ contributes nothing to it. Integration along $S$ multiplies integration along $C$ by $\delta$: $$\operatorname{pr}_{0,*}
      \widetilde{\operatorname{ch}}_2(E_{x(s)})=\delta e.$$ This follows directly from the degree formula $x_*[S]=\delta[C]$, or from the $C$-degree-two term in the Künneth decomposition. The other curve degrees integrate to zero. Combining this equality, the factor $2$ in <a href="#calc:middle-commutator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:middle-commutator">[calc:middle-commutator]</a>, and the self-intersection sign proves <a href="#calc:scalar" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:scalar">[calc:scalar]</a>.

The integer $\delta$ is positive because $x$ is a nonempty finite étale cover of connected projective curves. Thus <a href="#calc:f" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="calc:f">[calc:f]</a> is a nonzero rational rescaling and gives the stated double commutator. The degree of $f'$ is $-2$, since the two flag shifts cancel and integration over $S$ has degree $-2$. The filtration bounds are Propositions <a href="#spec:perverse-lowering" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:perverse-lowering">26</a> and <a href="#trans:weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="trans:weights">23</a>; they are unchanged by the rescaling. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:conclusion"></a>
### Equality of the filtrations

We assemble the preceding results on the same vector space $H^*(X,\mathbb Q)$, identified with $H^*(X^B,\mathbb Q)$ by nonabelian Hodge theory.

<details id="proof-stmt-calc-double-commutator-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-calc-double-commutator</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* First take $L=\mathcal O_C(dx_0)$. Proposition <a href="#norm:relative-lefschetz" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="norm:relative-lefschetz">4</a> gives hard Lefschetz for cup product by $e$ on $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}^P H^*(X)$, with grading shift two and center $R$. Section <a href="#sec:betti" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:betti">3</a> gives the same statement on $\mathop{\mathrm{Gr}}^W H^*(X^B)$ when actual weight $2k$ is assigned grade $k$. It also proves that all odd weight-graded pieces vanish.

Let $\delta=\deg(S\to C)>0$ and set $$f=\frac{(-1)^{b+1}}{4n(n-2)!\,\delta}\,f'.$$ Propositions <a href="#trans:weights" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="trans:weights">23</a> and <a href="#spec:perverse-lowering" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="spec:perverse-lowering">26</a> show that $f$ lowers the half-weight filtration and the perverse filtration by two. The calculation in Section <a href="#sec:commutator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:commutator">8</a> gives $[[f,e],e]=-2e$. Apply Proposition <a href="#alg:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="alg:comparison">5</a> to $P_k$ and $W_{2k}$. It follows that $P_kH^m(X)=W_{2k}H^m(X^B)$ in every cohomological degree. The vanishing of odd weight-graded pieces gives $W_{2k}=W_{2k+1}$.

For arbitrary $L\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$, choose $M\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)$ with $M^n\simeq L\otimes\mathcal O_C(-dx_0)$. Tensoring by $M$ identifies the Higgs spaces over the Hitchin base. Proposition <a href="#log:nah" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="log:nah">19</a> proves that this identification transports the full Betti weight filtration as well. This proves the theorem for every determinant. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-auto-corollary-1"></a>
<div class="corollary">

**Corollary 30**. *Let $C$ be a smooth projective complex curve of genus $g\ge2$, let $n\ge2$ and $d$ be coprime integers, and let $L\in\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^d(C)$. For the moduli space $X$ of stable rank-$n$, determinant-$L$, trace-free Higgs bundles and its corresponding twisted $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n$ character variety $X^B$, nonabelian Hodge theory identifies $$P_k H^m(X,\mathbb Q)=W_{2k}H^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)=W_{2k+1}H^m(X^B,\mathbb Q)
 \qquad\text{for all integers }m,k.$$ The equality holds on full rational cohomology, including its $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$-variant part.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-auto-corollary-1"><summary>Proof of stmt-auto-corollary-1</summary>

*Proof.* The composite ranks are Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>; the prime ranks are (Maulik and Shen 2024, Theorem 0.3) <a id="pm-cite-57"></a>[[MS2024](#ref-MS2024)]. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-endoscopic-weights"></a>
<div id="cor:endoscopic-weights" class="corollary">

**Corollary 31** (Endoscopic weight compatibility). *Keep $C,n,d,L$ as in the preceding corollary. Put $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$, let $\gamma\in\Gamma$ have order $m$, and let $\kappa\in\widehat{\Gamma}$ be the character $\kappa(\gamma')=\langle\gamma,\gamma'\rangle_\Gamma$ defined by the Weil pairing in (Maulik and Shen 2021, sec. 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-58"></a>[[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)]. Let $\pi:C'\to C$ be the associated cyclic étale cover, set $r=n/m$, and put $$d_\gamma=\operatorname{codim}_{\mathcal A^{K_C}}\mathcal A_\gamma^{K_C},$$ where $\mathcal A_\gamma^{K_C}$ is their endoscopic Hitchin base. For $D=K_C$ and $D'=\pi^*K_C=K_{C'}$, let $\mathfrak p_\kappa^B$ denote their nonabelian-Hodge-transported operator in (Maulik and Shen 2021, sec. 5.4) <a id="pm-cite-59"></a>[[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)]. Write $\widetilde{\mathcal M}_{r,d}^B(C')$ for the full degree-$d$ twisted $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_r$ character variety and $\mathcal M_{n,L}^B(C)=X^B$ for the fixed-determinant twisted $\mathop{\mathrm{SL}}_n$ character variety. On complexified cohomology, for all integers $i,k\ge0$, $$\begin{gathered}
\mathfrak p_\kappa^B\!\left(W_{2k}H^i(\widetilde{\mathcal M}_{r,d}^B(C'),\mathbb C)\right)\\
=W_{2k+2d_\gamma}H^{i+2d_\gamma}(\mathcal M_{n,L}^B(C),\mathbb C)_\kappa,
\end{gathered}$$ where the subscript $\kappa$ denotes the pullback-action summand on which $\gamma'^*\alpha=\kappa(\gamma')\alpha$ for every $\gamma'\in\Gamma$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-endoscopic-weights"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-endoscopic-weights</summary>

*Proof.* Since $r\mid n$, one has $\gcd(r,d)=1$, and étale Riemann–Hurwitz gives $g(C')=m(g-1)+1\ge2$. The all-rank $\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}_r$ theorem (Maulik and Shen 2024, Theorem 0.2) <a id="pm-cite-60"></a>[[MS2024](#ref-MS2024)] therefore identifies $W_{2k}$ with $P_k$ on the source, including when $r=1$. The preceding corollary identifies $P_{k+d_\gamma}$ with $W_{2k+2d_\gamma}$ on the target after complexification and passage to the $\Gamma$-$\kappa$ summand: the $\Gamma$-action preserves both filtrations, and the target nonabelian-Hodge identification is $\Gamma$-equivariant. With the common defect normalization, (Maulik and Shen 2021, Theorem 5.4) <a id="pm-cite-61"></a>[[MSEndoscopy2021](#ref-MSEndoscopy2021)] gives the exact image of $P_kH^i$ as $P_{k+d_\gamma}H^{i+2d_\gamma}_\kappa$. Writing $\eta_s,\eta_t$ for the source and target nonabelian-Hodge cohomology identifications from Betti to Dolbeault, the definition $\mathfrak p_\kappa^B=\eta_t^{-1}\mathfrak p_\kappa\eta_s$ then gives the claim. Its nonzero scalar ambiguity does not affect images. ◻

</details>

The standard implication from $P=W$ to perverse multiplicativity (M. A. de Cataldo, Maulik, and Shen 2022, sec. 0.4.3, equation (10)) <a id="pm-cite-62"></a>[[dCMSJAMS2022](#ref-dCMSJAMS2022)] now applies to the full fixed-determinant cohomology.

<a id="stmt-cor-perverse-multiplicativity"></a>
<div id="cor:perverse-multiplicativity" class="corollary">

**Corollary 32** (Multiplicativity of the perverse filtration). *In the setting of the all-rank corollary above, use the normalization <a href="#intro:P" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:P">[intro:P]</a>. For all $i,j\ge0$ and $a,b\in\mathbb Z$, $$P_aH^i(X,\mathbb Q)\smile P_bH^j(X,\mathbb Q)
\subseteq P_{a+b}H^{i+j}(X,\mathbb Q).$$ Put $\Gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}^0(C)[n]$ and $P_aH^i(X,\mathbb C)=P_aH^i(X,\mathbb Q)\otimes_{\mathbb Q}\mathbb C$. For the pullback-action isotypic parts indexed by $\chi,\psi\in\widehat\Gamma
=\operatorname{Hom}(\Gamma,\mathbb C^\times)$, one also has $$\bigl(P_aH^i(X,\mathbb C)\bigr)_\chi\smile
\bigl(P_bH^j(X,\mathbb C)\bigr)_\psi
\subseteq\bigl(P_{a+b}H^{i+j}(X,\mathbb C)\bigr)_{\chi\psi}.$$ This includes trivial and distinct characters, with the same normalized $P$ on every character summand.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-perverse-multiplicativity"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-perverse-multiplicativity</summary>

*Proof.* The nonabelian-Hodge diffeomorphism induces a graded ring isomorphism $\eta:H^*(X^B,\mathbb Q)\to H^*(X,\mathbb Q)$ (Hausel and Thaddeus 2003, sec. 2 and 5) <a id="pm-cite-63"></a>[[HT2003](#ref-HT2003)]. Cup product is a morphism of mixed Hodge structures (Deligne 1974, Corollaire (8.2.11)) <a id="pm-cite-64"></a>[[Deligne1974](#ref-Deligne1974)], so it sends $W_{2a}H^i(X^B,\mathbb Q)\smile W_{2b}H^j(X^B,\mathbb Q)$ into $W_{2(a+b)}H^{i+j}(X^B,\mathbb Q)$. Transport by $\eta$ and the all-rank $P=W$ equality give the first assertion. The filtration is $\Gamma$-stable, and $\gamma^*(\alpha\smile\beta)=\gamma^*\alpha\smile\gamma^*\beta$ multiplies the two eigencharacters, giving the second assertion. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The scalar correspondence. All moving-pole spaces retain the curve $S$; $pr$ has fiber $S$, $π$ is a flag projection of relative dimension $b$, and $ι$ has codimension $b$. The residue on $Y_{λ, 0}$ may have a strictly flag-lowering nilpotent part in addition to its prescribed scalar part; $Z_{λ}$ requires that nilpotent part to vanish. The derived morphisms and their cohomological and diagram-weight shifts are recorded in Table 1.",
      "figure_id": "log:scalar-correspondence",
      "pdf_page": 19,
      "source_evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "The scalar correspondence. All moving-pole spaces retain the\ncurve $S$; $\\operatorname{pr}$ has fiber $S$, $\\pi$ is a\nflag projection of relative dimension $b$, and $\\iota$ has codimension\n$b$. The residue on $Y_{\\lambda,0}$ may have a stri",

      "source_kind": "other",
      "tikz_pgf_source": null,
      "witness_description": "A horizontal diagram showing four moduli spaces connected by arrows:\n- $X_\\lambda \\xleftarrow{\\mathrm{pr}} \\mathcal{O}_\\lambda \\simeq S \\times X_\\lambda$ with a left-pointing arrow labeled $\\mathrm{pr}$\n- $\\mathcal{O}_\\lambda \\simeq S \\times X_\\lambda \\xleftarrow{\\pi} \\mathcal{Z}_\\lambda$ with a left-pointing arrow labeled $\\pi$\n- $\\mathcal{Z}_\\lambda \\xrightarrow{\\iota} Y_{\\lambda,0}$ with a right-pointing arrow labeled $\\iota$"
    }
  ],
